## CHAPTER XXX On [[Mināmsā|Mīmāṁsā]] principles and rules of interpretation in relation to [[Dharmasastra|Dharmaśāstra]]

[[P1283]]The [[Pārvamimamas ayolyed|Pūrvamīmāṁsā evolved]] a method of its own and a set of principles for the interpretation of Vedic texts. It is now time to [[get|set]] down and explain some of these principles and [[rulea|rules]] and point out how they have been employed by [[Dharmasastra|Dharmaśāstra]] writers for solving their own difficulties. 

The [[Mimārsā|Mīmāṁsā]] principles and rules of interpretation fall into several groups. There are rules that are concerned only with the details of Vedic [[saorifices|sacrifices]] and their inter-relations. The most important rule in this field is this that only _Vidhis_ properly so called have absolute authority and binding force, while what are called _arthavādas_ have authority in so far only as they form one syntactical whole with the vidhis and as they are meant to praise the vidhis. (P. M. [[8.|S.]] I. [[2.7).|2. 7).]] Vidhis and Arthavādas are not discussed consecutively, but they are scattered over the several chapters of the P. M. S. For example, arthavādas are first dealt with in I. [[%.|2.]] 1-18 (the [[arthavadādhi karaña|arthavādādhikaraṇa]]), but in many other places there is a discussion about arthavādas as in III. 4. 1-9, [[II, 4, 10,|III. 4. 10,]] III. 4. 11, IV. 3. 1-3, VI. 7. 26–30, X. 8. 5, X. 8. 7 and 8. 

It should not be forgotten that the [[Mimarisi|Mīmāṁsā]] is not [[concer ned|concerned]] with legislation by the king or [[&|a]] sovereign popular assembly. It promises to convey [[Correct|correct]] knowledge of Dharma (meaning religious rites and matters connected therewith) and the means of arriving at that [[knowldge|knowledge]] is the Veda itself and the main purpose of the [[Mimāṁsa|Mīmāṁsā]] is to regulate the procedure (_itikartavyatā_), the various auxiliary and principal matters in Vedic sacrifices.[^2094] 

There is a great deal of difference between the [[interprota tion|interpretation]] of statutes and the [[Mimāṁsā|Mīmāṁsā]] rules of interpretation. In the 

[^2094]: [[धर्म|धर्मे]] [[प्रमीयमाणे तु घेवेन करणात्मना। इतिकर्तग्यताभार्ग मीमांसा पूरयिष्यति । g. by greengroft on neqat (p.36). s ipentuelt of more p.3, PAGE4.|प्रमीयमाणे तु वेदेन करणात्मना । इतिकर्तव्यताभागं मीमांसा पूरयिष्यति ॥ q. by युक्तिस्नेहपूरणी on शास्त्रदीपिका (p. 36), अधिकरणकौमुदी of देवनाथ p. 3, तन्त्ररहस्य.]] The word _itikartavyatā_ occurs in P. M. S. itself (III. 3. 11 [[sag op faranno ader|असंयुक्तं प्रकरणादितिकर्तव्यतार्थत्वात्]]). On the preceding sūtra ([[Tanga|भूयस्त्वेनोभयश्रुति]]) [[744|शबर]] remarks [['\\#art Ynt: Affirmall'|'ये च भूयांसो गुणाः सेतिकर्तव्यता',]] and on P. M. S. XI. 2. 8 ([[ora o Furtappe अधानेनोपविश्यरतस्मात्स्यायेकवेशल|अङ्गानि तु विधानस्यात्प्रधानेनोपदिश्येरंस्तस्मात्स्यादेकदेशत्वम्]]) [[पर|शबर]] explains [['विधानं काल्प इतिकर्तव्यतेत्यर्थः।|'विधानं कल्प इतिकर्तव्यतेत्यर्थः'.]] 

[[P1284]]first place, statutes are man-made, they express the will of the [[enaoting|enacting]] authority, have mostly secular purposes, may be amended or even repealed and have to be expounded according to the intent of those that made them. But the [[Mimamsa is Concerned|Mīmāṁsā is concerned]] with the Veda that is deemed eternal and [[self-eristent|self-existent]] (and not [[man-made )|man-made),]] that deals with religious matters, cannot be amended or repealed and is to be expounded according to the intent of the Vedic words. Therefore, though some rules of the interpretation of Vedic texts evolved by the Pūrvamīmāṁsā are identical with or resemble the rules of the interpretation of statutes developed in such works as Maxwell's on 'interpretation of statutes', the author will not enter into any detailed [[treat ment|treatment]] showing the parallelisms [[betwood Mlmāngā|between Mīmāṁsā]] rules and Maxwell's rules. This task was attempted over fifty years ago by Mr. Kishorilal Sarkar in his Tagore Law [[Lecturos|Lectures]] published in 1909. It would appear ungracious on the part of the present author to offer criticisms against a predecessor in the same field who wrote more than half a century ago when [[Mimāmgā|Mīmāṁsā]] studies by modern educated Indians were in their [[infanoy|infancy]]. But the author cannot help stating that Mr. Sarkar was [[obsesed|obsessed]] by the notion that he must show somehow or other that Jaimini's rules of interpretation were not inferior to those of Maxwell's and agreed closely with him. For this purpose he relies often on far-fetched analogies and employs obscure explanations. In several cases it appears that he had not been able to grasp correctly what Jaimini and [[Sabara|Śabara]] meant.[^2095] 

In this work the author is concerned only with the question of the influence on Dharmaśāstra of Pūrvamīmāṁsā [[dootrines|doctrines]] and rules of interpretation. He has already shown how many of the [[dootrines|doctrines]] and technical expressions of the Mīmāṁsā have dominated [[Dharmagāstra|Dharmaśāstra]]. He would now turn to the rules of interpretation. 

The first rule is that no part of the Veda (not even a word) can be treated as _anarthaka_ (meaningless or purposeless). It is therefore that a very large part of the Veda is treated [[28 artha vadas|as arthavādas]] intended to praise vidhis. This has been stated above (p. 1243). The result of the vital importance attached to vidhis 

[^2095]: Vide [[bis|his]] explanation of the words [[' • Vedam kftvā|'Vedam kṛtvā]] vedim karoti' in pp. 241-242 of his work and the remarks of the present author [[la H, of Db.|in H. of Dh.]] Vol. III, pp. 841-42 and his explanation of 'arthakarma' and 'pratipattikarma' as : 'essential act' and [['200-essential|'non-essential]] act' respectively [[(Pp.|(pp.]] 186-187) and the explanations of these given above on pp. 1231-32. 

[[P1285]]in the PMS and of the assignment of a very subordinate role to _arthavādas_ (as merely intended to praise) and to mantras (as merely 'abhidhāyaka') has resulted in this that a small portion of the Brāhmaṇas has been held to be of supreme authority, while a large part of the Brāhmaṇas and of [[Sarhitās contain ing|Saṁhitās containing]] mantras has become of secondary importance or of little importance. 

The [[Mimāmgā|Mīmāṁsā]] rules of interpretation fall into different classes from different standpoints. Some rules are general and some are special. Some rules have to do with the proper [[proce dure|procedure]] to be followed when several texts laying down matters relating to the same subject are in conflict by means of the application of the technical words _śruti_, _liṅga_, _vākya_, [[prakar ana|prakaraṇa]], [[athāna|sthāna]] and _samākhyā_ (III. 3. 14) and there are rules about [[adhikara|adhikāra]], _atideśa_, _ūha_, _bādha_, _tantra_ and _prasaṅga_. 

Some examples of general rules may first be cited. That _vidhis_ alone have special obligatory authority and that _arthavādas_ are authoritative only in so far as they form one syntactical whole with the vidhis is a general rule. The rules distinguishing between _vidhis_ (properly so called), _niyama vidhis_ and _parisaṅkhyā_ are [[general|general.]] 

The [[Mimāmgā|Mīmāṁsā]] rule is that where there is a[^2096] doubt about the exact meaning of a part of a passage, it may be determined by relying on the remaining parts of the [[pasbage|passage]]. Vide above for the example 'he puts down anointed pebbles' &c. (p. 1240). The Mīmāṁsā is very much against assuming conflict among Vedic texts and therefore allows an option only if there is no other way out. Vide remarks on _vikalpa_ above (p. 1250). Another general rule is that the singular includes the plural. In the [[Mimāmgā|Mīmāṁsā]] it is called 'Grahaikatvanyāya (P. M. S. [[II.|III.]] 1. 13-15). In the Jyotiṣṭoma sacrifice a number of cups (_grahas_) filled with Soma are offered to deities and drunk at the three _savanas_ (pressings of Soma) and Śruti states 'he wipes or cleanses the [[oup|cup]] with the strainer made of white wool' ([[daśapavitrona|daśāpavitreṇa]] [[graham sam-mārsti|grahaṁ sam-mārṣṭi]]) and in the [[Dargapūrṇamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] it is said 'he carries a firebrand round the [[purodāsa|puroḍāśa]] (cake)'. The question is 

[^2096]: [[peanut 979984 y. 7. 8.|सन्दिग्धेषु वाक्यशेषात् । पू. मी. सू.]] I. 4. 24. The [[fare|विषयवाक्य]] is [[' refier ou una Ast er (. T.|'अक्ताः शर्करा उपदधाति तेजो वै घृतम्' (तै. ब्रा.]] III. 2. 5. 12). Compare Maxwell p. 29 [['the words of each clause should be so interpreted as to bring them in barmony with other provisions.'|'the words of each clause should be so interpreted as to bring them in harmony with other provisions.']] 

[[P1286]]whether a single [[oup|cup]] ('graham' is the word used) is to be cleansed or a single [[purodāsa|puroḍāśa]] is to have a firebrand carried round it or whether all cups called [[grabas or purodagas|grahas or puroḍāśas]] are to be treated as stated. The established conclusion is that all grahas are to be cleansed and all [[purodāsas|puroḍāśas]] have the firebrand carried round them. The singular number is not intended to be insisted on.[^2097] From this a further general rule is derived by Kumārila and others that an attribute of the subject (_anuvādya_ or [[uddiśyamūna|uddiśyamāna]])[^2098] about which something is predicated (_vidheya_) is not intended and should not be insisted upon. This is [[fro quently|frequently]] relied on in Dharmaśāstra works. Yāj. II. 121 states that the father and son have equal ownership in land, [[&|a]] corrody and wealth (gold and silver &c.) [[aoquired|acquired]] by the grand-father. Here the word [[pilāmaha|pitāmaha]] is not to be insisted upon and the same rule applies to land or wealth acquired by a great-grand-father, as the V. M. says.[^2099] Similarly, when [[Nāradasmrti|Nāradasmṛti]] says [[( 16. 37)|(16. 37)]] 'of brothers being undivided religious worship (or rites) are one, but if there is a partition then they may have separate religious worship'. Here the proper subject is the word 'undivided persons', and the word 'brothers' is used only as an attribute, which is not intended to be insisted on and the same rule applies to undivided grand-father, father, sons, uncles and [[nepbows|nephews]]. Medhātithi on Manu [[II, 29 montions|II. 29 mentions]] this maxim. The same rule is applied in some cases to gender also i. e. a word importing males includes females also. For example, Yāj. II. 182 and Nārada (8. 40 ff) lay down certain rules about a _dāsa_ (male [[glave|slave]]). The V. M. says that the masculine gender in [[those|these]] passages is not intended to be insisted upon, but the rules

[[P1287]]apply even to female [[slaves. 100|slaves.]][^2100] There are exceptions to these rules. The rule about _grahas_ does not apply to _camasas_ (P. M. S. III. 1. [[16–17). 2001|16-17)].[^2101]] This rule that the qualification ([[viboṣana|viśeṣana]]) of the subject in a _vidhi_ is not meant to be taken literally and emphasized is applied in many other cases. Three verses from the [[Kālikāpurāpa|Kālikāpurāṇa]] are quoted by Kalpataru (on vyavahāra pp. 210-211) and V. M. (pp. 45-46) about the performance of an ordeal in certain grave charges and this maxim is applied by the V. M. there in the words '[[paradārarūpam viśeṣanam-avivakṣitam abhisāpasyānuvādyatvāt'|paradārarūpaṁ viśeṣaṇam-avivakṣitam-abhiśāpasyānuvādyatvāt]];' vide notes to V. M. pp. 83-84. But in 'paśum-ālabheta', where there is a vidhi about 'yāga it must be held that what is laid down is yāga as qualified by being made with a male animal and therefore only one paśu (and that [[pasu|paśu]] a [[male )|male)]] is to be sacrificed. 

Although the Veda employs the masculine gender (in [[gvargakāmo yajota|svargakāmo yajeta]], 'one desiring heaven should offer a sacrifice'), yet Jaimini establishes (in VI. 1. 6-16) that even women are included and have a right to offer a yāga.[^2102] Jaimini further provides that husband and wife should perform a religious [[right|rite]] in co-operation (VI. 1. 17-21), but he provides that where the Śruti specifically provides for certain matters to be done by the yajamāna[^2103] (the male performer) it is the male alone who can perform them as the wife is not equal to the husband in the knowledge of mantras and is ignorant and therefore she is restricted to the performance of those acts that are expressly enjoined for her such as looking at the clarified butter, observing _brahmacarya_ and the like (VI. 1. 24 '[[tasyā yāvad-uktam-āsirbra hmacaryam-atulyatvāt'|tasyā yāvad-uktam-āśīrbrahmacaryam-atulyatvāt]]'.) The wife also performs bath and such

[[P1288]]acts as applying [[collyrium,] sipping|collyrium, sipping]] water ([[acamana|(ācamana)]]), observes [[silenca|silence]] till the morning or evening agnihotra is gone through. She has her waist girt up with [[&|a]] yoktra (a triple cord of [[muftja|muñja]] grass) in [[Darsapūrṇamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] and other sacrifices. She has also to look upon the clarified butter in the pot with a mantra [[(Tai 8.IL). 10.3.mahinām payosyoṣadhinām rasossi adabdhena tvā cakugas vek se suprajāstyāya'|(Tai. S. II. 10. 3 'mahīnāṁ payosyoṣadhīnāṁ raso'ssi adabdhena tvā cakṣuṣāvekṣe suprajāstvāya')]] ('thou art the milk of cows, the fluid of [[borbs|herbs]], with an invincible eye I look on thee for securing good progeny'). The wife was to learn the mantras she had to repeat in sacrifices from her father or husband before the husband set up the sacred fires (vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. p. 1041 n). [[Gradua lly|Gradually]], the wife lost all importance in Vedic sacrifices and came to be a [[more|mere]] silent spectator[^2104] of all the weary details that had to be gone through by the sacrificer and the priest. 

In spite of the above restrictions on the wife's powers about Vedic sacrifices many [[empti|smṛti]] rules apply to women also, though the text employs the masculine gender. For example, [[Mapu|Manu]] XI. 93 provides that a [[brābmapa, ksatriya and vaisya|brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya and vaiśya]] should not drink surā. This prohibition applies to wives of the [[mombers|members]] of the three varṇas according to the Mit. on Yāj. [[II.|III.]] 256. 

The P. M. S. holds that the gender and number of a word in a [[vidhitūkya|vidhivākya]] may in [[cortain casos|certain cases]] be intended and insisted on. For example, in PMS IV. 1. 11-16 it is established that the [[Agni. · somlya|Agniṣomīya]] animal to be sacrificed in [[Jyotiṣtoma 88|Jyotiṣṭoma as]] prescribed in the words '[[yo dikṣito yad agnisomlyam paśum-alabhati|yo dīkṣito yad agnīṣomīyaṁ paśum-ālabhati]]' (that the person who has undergone [[dikṣā|dīkṣā]] and offers the animal to Agni and Soma) is only one and that the words in the [[Aßve medha|Aśvamedha]] 'he offers kapiñjalas to the season of[^2105] spring', the

This is [[f orgate|कपिञ्जलन्याय]] embodied in [[. . .|पू. मी. सू.]] XI. 1. 31-46. The plural [[Kapiõjalān|Kapiñjalān]] is satisfied by offering three as the lowest [[bomber.|number.]] To offer a thousand would not yield more [[abandast|abundant]] reward, since only a single provision is made and not several alternate [[aombers.|numbers.]] The [[शामदीपिका|शास्त्रदीपिका]] remarks [['यो हि श्रीनालभते यश्च सह सयोरभयोरपि बहुलसम्पादनमा विशिष्टम्।... निवृत्तव्यापारे च विधौ, न हिस्यादिति निषेधशा प्रवर्तत इत्यधिकामालम्मा।'|'यो हि त्रीनालभते यश्च सहस्रं तयोरुभयोरपि बहुत्वसम्पादनमाविशिष्टम् । ... निवृत्तव्यापारे च विधौ, न हिंस्यादिति निषेधशास्त्रं प्रवर्तत इत्यधिकानामालम्भः ।']] It is referred to in [[4. #.|परा. मा.]] I. 2. 281 [[' roror fia van toerera form AFATIT Papa: Fortura: grua radii'.|' प्राणायामैरिति बहुवचनस्य कपिञ्जलन्यायेन त्रित्वे पर्यवसानात् त्रिभिः प्राणायामैः शुध्यति इत्यर्थः ।'.]] Compare also P. M. S. IV. 1. 11 ff.; [['aure forgog .|'तथा च लिङ्गम्' पू. मी. सू.]] IV. 1. 17. The [[Passage|passage]] is [[8. .|तै. सं.]] II. 1. 2. 5 [['Thirat मावरायी कृष्णबीवीमालमेत बीम्ने मापन्दिने संहितामेलीं शपपराडेतोपाईस्पस्याम्'|'वसन्ते मातरायीं कृष्णग्रीवीमालभेत ग्रीष्मे मध्यन्दिने संहितानामेन्द्रीं शरद्यपराह्ने श्वेतां बार्हस्पत्याम्']] and then in [[d. #.|तै. सं.]] II. 1. 2. 6 [['triturat wafia, futro ningunha ref.:Kapinjaladhikarana|'गर्भिण्यो भवन्ति, इन्द्रियं वै गर्भ इन्द्रियमेवास्मिन् दधति. ']] 

[[P1289]]kapiñjala birds to be offered are only three (and not one nor two nor more than three). Similarly, the gender in the passage 'he sacrifices in the morning in spring an animal with dark coloured neck to Agni, in summer in the noon an animal of different colours, in the autumn in the afternoon [[&|a]] white animal to [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]]', it is a female animal that is offered because [[immedia tely|immediately]] thereafter the words 'they become pregnant' occur. Dharmaśāstra works often say that the masculine gender used in many texts excludes women. For example, the Agnipurāṇa[^2106] (175. 59-61), when dealing with the rules to be observed in all [[vrataa|vratas]] in general, provides that the person undergoing [[prata|vrata]] should take a bath, should worship the golden images of the deities of the vratas, perform japa and homa and at the end of the vrata make gifts according to his ability and should feed 24, 12, 5 or only three vipras. The [[N, S.|N. S.]] quotes this from [[Pṛthylcandra|Pṛthvīcandra]] and observes that as the masculine gender 'viprāḥ' is employed only males of the brāhmaṇa class are to be [[fod|fed]] and not women. 

As against this, when Hemādri[^2107] quoting Padma says 'If a woman is pregnant, freshly delivered, or is ill and becomes impure, she should get her vrata performed through another and when she becomes pure she may [[obseryo|observe]] it personally,' the [[N. 8.|N. S.]] remarks that this applies to men also when they are impure, since the gender here is not intended to be emphasized. 

There are Mīmāṁsā rules about the interpretation of words and also about sentences. First a few of the rules about words may be illustrated. (1) Śabara in the very first sentence of his bhāṣya propounds the principle that as far as possible the words in the sūtras of Jaimini and in the Veda are to be understood in the sense in which they are understood in popular usage and not in a secondary or technical sense. The same rule is propounded by Jaimini in III. 2. 1-2 in connection with the meaning of 'barhis' in the mantra 'I cut the barhis as an abode for gods'. Here the conclusion is that barhis is to be taken in the primary sense of 'handful of [[kusa|kuśa]] grass' and not in a secondary sense

(04 go भय्यायाः|पृथ्वीचन्द्रोदयेऽग्निपुराणे—स्नात्वा व्रतवता सर्वव्रतेषु व्रतभूतंयः । पूज्याः सुवर्णमय्याद्याः]] ... व्रतान्ते [[वानमेव|दानमेव]] च । [[चतुविश...पशवाय|चतुर्विंश ... पञ्च वा त्रय]] एव च । विप्रा भोज्या यथाशक्ति [[तेग्यो वधाच|तेभ्यो दद्याच्च]] दक्षिणाम् ॥ अत्र [[विमा|विप्रा]] इति पुल्लिङ्गनिर्देशात् पुमांस एव भोज्याः, न [[चियः।|स्त्रियः ।]] [[pā Herriaren for RTTET ATT PASSTICI A. f.|एवं सहस्रभोजनादावपि । विरूपशोषस्य प्रमाणावरं विनाऽयुक्तत्वात् । नि. सिं.]] p. 24. It also [[ralles|relies]] on what [[Sabara|Śabara]] says on III. [[3, 17|3. 17]] and 19.

[[P1290]]such as kāśa or other kind of grass. [[Sabara windg|Śabara winds]] up with the[^2108] words 'between the primary and secondary senses of [[&|a]] word it is proper to hold that the primary sense is to be taken for the business in hand'. Again, on [[L|I.]] 3. 30 [[Sabara|Śabara]] says[^2109] that the words are the same as in the Veda and popular use and the meanings are the same. 

As regards the setting up of Vedic fires, the Tai. Br. I. 1. 4 and Āp. Śr. V. 3. 18 prescribe different seasons for men of the three varṇas and add that a 'rathakāra should set up Vedic [[firog|fires]] in the rainy [[season.|season'.]] The question arises whether the word 'rathakāra' in these passages means a person of that [[casto ( i.e.|caste (i. e.]] whether it is to be taken in the popular sense) or whether it refers to any one of any varṇa who manufactures chariots (i. e. the etymological sense). The established conclusion is that the popular sense is to be taken and not the etymological (P. M. S. VI. 1. 44-50). In the case of the rathakāra the mantra for ādhāna (setting up of Vedic fires) is [[';bhūṇām tva'(Tai. Br. I. 1. 4.8).|'ṛbhūṇāṁ tvā' (Tai. Br. I. 1. 4. 8).]] Though not belonging to one of the three higher [[varpas,|varṇas,]] the [[ratbakāra|rathakāra]] could repeat that mantra because the [[Sruti ex prosaly|Śruti expressly]] authorized him to do [[so. but|so, but]] he was not entitled to upanayana. The P. M. S. (VI. 1. 50) held that the rathakāra (mentioned in the Tai. Br. and Āp. Śr.) was a caste called Saudhanvana which is neither [[gūdra|śūdra]] nor one of the three higher varṇas but is slightly inferior to them. Vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. pp. 45–46. The [[S, Kau.|S. Kau.]] argues that[^2110] if once the right of a Hindu

B.)|(F. [[P1291]]widow to adopt is conceded, the [[more|mere]] fact that she is not entitled to repeat Vedic mantras in general cannot deprive her of that right and that it is possible to hold, as in the case of the [[ratha. kara|rathakāra]], that she can repeat the [[specifio|specific]] mantra required in [[adop ting|adopting]] a boy. The Tai. S. IV. 5. 4. 2 separately mentions several [[craftamen|craftsmen]] such as takṣan, rathakāra, kulāla, karmāra [[&o.|&c.]] From [[Atharvayeda III, 5. 6|Atharvaveda III. 5. 6]] and Vāj. S. 30. 6 (medhāyai [[rathakāram|rathakāraṁ]] dhairyāya takṣāṇam) it appears that the rathakāra held a good position in the then society. 

A word is to be understood in that shade of its meaning that is appropriate to the act in hand. For example, śruti says 'he cuts off with sruva, he cuts off with a knife, he cuts off with the hand' (the same verb 'avadyati' being used for all the acts). The question is whether one is to cut all offerings, whether liquid or solid, whether of flesh or other substances, with sruva ladle or whether he is to employ the method or implement appropriate for each i. e. clarified butter should be taken from [[&|a]] pot and offered by means of a [[sruya|sruva]] ladle, flesh should be [[out|cut]] off with a knife and offered, and hard or thick things (like fuel sticks) should be offered with the hand. The conclusion is that one is to offer in the way most appropriate to each kind of offering. This is called '[[samarthyādhikarana|sāmarthyādhikaraṇa]]' (PMS.[^2111] I. 4. 25). The V. M. relies on this when dealing with the homa [[proscribed|prescribed]] by [[Pitāmāba|Pitāmaha]] for all ordeals in the words 'homa should be offered in the four directions with clarified butter, boiled rice and fuel sticks &c' and [[deolares|declares]] that the homa of clarified butter should be made with [[gruva|sruva]] ladle, of havis (i. e. 'boiled [[rios|rice]] &c') with [[Bruc|sruc]] and of fuel [[sticka|sticks]] with the (right) hand, on account of the fitness ([[samarthya|sāmarthya]]) of these for the various offerings and sharply criticizes Raghunandana who in his Dāyatattva provided that the homa should be made of all three together (and not [[separate ly|separately]]). A similar rule is applied to the ten sacrificial implements 

[^2111]: [[sulet it rantai|अर्थाद्वा कल्पनेकदेशत्वात् ।]] [[g. fi. &.|पू. मी. सू.]] I. 4. 25; [[I quotesgramata,|शबर quotes 'स्रुवेणावद्यति,]] [[स्वाधतिनावयति हस्तेनावधति, इति भूयते । किं भवेणावदातव्यं सर्वस्व अवस्य संहवस्थ मासस्य च। तथा स्वधितिना हस्तेन च उत सर्वेषामर्थतो ज्यवस्था'.|स्वधितिनावद्यति हस्तेनावद्यति, इति श्रूयते । किं स्रुवेणावदातव्यं सर्वस्य द्रव्यस्य संहतस्य मांसस्य च । तथा स्वधितिना हस्तेन च उत सर्वेषामर्थतो व्यवस्था'.]] The [[पूर्वपकअविशेषा. foruraraquer.|पूर्वपक्ष is अविशेषाभिधानादव्यवस्टेति.]] The conclusion is [[spurgt FIFTTT, #TACYlanerara gorduar शकुपात् तथा यस्य शक्रयात् तस्य चेति । आख्यातशब्दानामर्थे अवता शकिः सहकारिणी।|अर्थाद्वा कल्पना, सामर्थ्यात्कल्पनेति स्रुवेणावद्येद्यथा शक्नुयात् तथा यस्य शक्नुयात् तस्य चेति । आख्यातशब्दानामर्थे ब्रुवतां शक्तिः सहकारिणी ।]] [[एवं वेद्यथाशक्ति|एवं चेद्यथाशक्ति]] व्यवस्था भवितुमर्हति । तथा [[अखालिना सक्कल प्रवान्ये|अञ्जलिना सक्तून् प्रदाव्ये]] जुहोति इति ।. [[Thle|This]] last is a quotation from Tait. S. III. 3. 8. Vide [[69. 8.|व्य. म.]] p. 54 for the text and notes pp. 89-93 for detailed explanations of the passage by the [[prosent|present]] author. The [[शानदीपिका|शास्त्रदीपिका]] on the above remarks [['तस्माच्छकिसहायो विधिरेव पया सामय विधेयं ज्यवस्थापयति.'|'तस्माच्छक्तिसहायो विधिरेव यथासामर्थ्यं विधेयं व्यवस्थापयति.']]

[[P1292]]enumerated in Tai. S. I. 6. [[8, 2 vis.|8. 2 viz.]] sphya (the wooden sword), the potsherds [[&o.|&c.]] Here the [[Purvapakṣa|Pūrvapakṣa]] is that one may employ any one of these for any [[purposo|purpose]] required in the sacrifice; the established conclusion (PMS [[III.1 11.|III. 1. 11.]] and [[IV, 1.7-10|IV. 1. 7-10]]) is that the enumeration of ten implements is a [[more|mere]] _anuvāda_ and is not to be understood as the pūrvapakṣa states, but each is to be employed for the purpose for which the Vedic texts prescribe it (such as one cooks the [[purodāsa|puroḍāśa]] on potsherds, pounds grains in mortar and [[pastle|pestle]]). Vide H. of Dh. vol. II. p. 985 n. 2233 for [[ton|ten]] implements of sacrifice and others that are required and p. 1232 above for their disposal. 

One and the same word must not be used in two [[songos|senses]] in the same sentence, that is, in the primary sense and also in the figurative sense.[^2112] This maxim is relied upon by the [[Day&. bhāga|Dāyabhāga]]. When brothers (sons of the same mother) come to [[&|a]] partition, smṛti texts (like Yāj. II. 123) [[proscribe|prescribe]] that the mother takes [[&|a]] share equal to that of a son. On this the [[Dāyabhaga|Dāyabhāga]] remarks that the word '[[mata|mātā]]' (in Yāj. II. 123 and others) primarily means a real mother (_[[janani,|jananī,]]_ that gives birth) and this smṛti rule has no application to the step-mother, since it is improper to hold that the same word once used in a sentence has a primary and a secondary sense at the same[^2113] time. But [[It|it]] must be pointed out that all Dharmaśāstra works do not observe this rule. Aparārka (p. 730) on Yāj. II. 123 [[(pitur-ārd hyam vibhajatām tātāpyamsam samam baret)|(pitur-ūrdhvam vibhajatāṁ mātāpyaṁśaṁ samaṁ haret)]] includes the [[step mother|step-mother]] under the word 'mātā' following a text of [[Vyāsa)|Vyāsa.]] The [[Mitakṣarā|Mitākṣarā]] interpreting Yāj. II. 135 about obstructible heritage stating the order of succession as wife, daughters, parents, brothers, their sons, provides that full brothers first succeed, in default of them half brothers, in default of the last, full brothers' 

[^2112]: [[rarearancang I 79 00|अन्यायश्चानेकार्थत्वम् । शबर on]] III. 2. 1 and [[VII, 3. 3;|VII. 3. 3;]] [[# TY merrettual moet het rartat i punt On|न ह्येकस्य शब्दस्यानेकार्थता सत्यां गतो युक्ता । शबर on]] VIII. 3. 22. Vide [[pret op|शबर on]] IX. 4. 18 [[Alno.|also.]] [[tard in bis bbāsya on Brabpasūtra|शङ्कराचार्य in his bhāṣya on Brahmasūtra]] II. 4. 3 puts this [[rale|rule]] very clearly [['नोकस्मिन्प्रकरणे एकस्मिश्च वाक्ये एकः शब्दः सफचरितो बहुभिः सम्पायमाना काचिन्मुख्यः क्वचिद् गौण इत्यव्यवसात शत्रयम्। वैशाप्यमसात् ।.|'न ह्येकस्मिन्प्रकरणे एकस्मिंश्च वाक्ये एकः शब्दः सकृदुच्चरितो बहुभिः सम्बध्यमानः क्वचिन्मुख्यः क्वचिद् गौण इत्यध्यवसातुं शक्यम् । वैरूप्यप्रसङ्गात् ।'.]] 

[^2113]: पितरि चोपरते [[सोदरम्रातृभिविभागे|सोदरभ्रातृभिर्विभागे]] क्रियमाणे [[मापि|मात्रेपि]] [[पुत्रसमाशो दातम्या|पुत्रसमांशो दातव्यः]] समांशहारिणी मातेति [[पचनात्|वचनात्]] । [[मातुपवस्य जननीपरत्वास सपनीमातपरत्वमपि सक an outorgaretier|मातृपदस्य जननीपरत्वान्न सपत्नीमातृपरत्वमपि सकृच्छ्रुतस्य मुख्यगौणत्वानुपपत्तेः । दायभाग]] [[III29–30, p.67;|III. 29-30, p. 67;]] [[Argugut akuratastardai muk|मातृग्रहणं तत्सपल्यादिप्रदर्शनार्थम् । अपरार्क]] p. 730. It may be noted that [[lo|in]] Vithal V. Prahlad [[(I, L, R.|(I. L. R.]] 39 Bom. 373) [[. paternal|a paternal]] step-grandmother was held entitled to a [[sbaro wbon *|share when a]] partition took place between the sons of a deceased son of [[bar co-wito.|her co-wife.]] 

[[P1293]][[BODA.|sons.]] The V. M. disagrees[^2114] with this and says that the word 'brother' primarily means 'full brother' and only secondarily 'half-brother', that, as it is not allowable to use a word like brother in two [[sonses|senses]] in the same passage, in default of full brother his son succeeds (and not half-brother as the Mit. provides). The primary sense of a word is got by 'abhidhā', the secondary [[sonde|sense]] by 'lakṣaṇā'[^2115] and sometimes a third sense called suggested sense is said to be due to vyañjanā. These are the three [[vỹttis|vṛttis]] (functions) of a word. 

One of the rules about the interpretations of words is contained in P. M. S. I. 3. 8-9. [[Sabara|Śabara]] cites three instances of words[^2116] viz. caru made from yavas, sandals made of boar skin, a cane mat. The words yava, varāha and vetasa are respectively employed by some people in the sense of '[[priyangu|priyaṅgu]]' (long pepper), dark bird (crow) and jambu ([[black berry|blackberry]]). The prima facie [[viow|view]] insists that one may use [[those|these]] words in any one of the two senses. The Siddhānta is that [[those|these]] words must be used in the [[songe|sense]] in which the Veda (or śāstra) or [[sistas|śiṣṭas]] employ them i. e. the usage of learned Aryan people is to be followed where words

[[4, 12.|4. 12.]] [[Toret atq:|गङ्गायां घोषः]] is [[op (Tetant alt:),|लक्षणा (गङ्गातीरे घोषः),]] while [[aruta:|अग्निर्माणवकः]] (the boy is fire) is an example of [[tot grot|गौणीवृत्ति]] (possession of a quality common to both). [[Filoft|गौणी]] is a variety of [[non|लक्षणा]] which latter occurs in a far larger number of cases. The boy has some of the qualities [[(90)|(गुण)]] of fire such as very brown colour and the like and therefore here the word 'fire' is applied to the [[bor|boy]] in a figurative sense. 

[^2116]: [[Borgstantiadero Ft Farrage: Fire & &|तेष्वदर्शनाद्विरोधस्य समा विप्रतिपत्तिः स्यात् । पू. मी. सू.]] I. 3. 8; [[:, पाराही उपानहो, पैतसे कटे प्राजापत्यान सचिनोति इति यववराहवेतसशब्दान समाममन्ति । नत्र फेचिदीर्षकेषु यशवं प्रयुखते केचित्प्रियास। पराहशद केचिएकरे केचित्कृष्णशकुनो। बेवसशष्य केचित्रशुलके केचिजम्बाम्। शबर,|यवमयश्चरुः, वाराही उपानहौ, वैतसे कटे प्राजापत्यान् संचिनोति इति यववराहवेतसशब्दान् समामनन्ति । तत्र केचिद्दीर्घशूकेषु यवशब्दं प्रयुञ्जते केचित्प्रियङ्गुषु । वराहशब्दं केचित्सूकरे केचित्कृष्णशकुनौ । वेतसशब्दं केचिद्वञ्जुलके केचिज्जाम्बवे । शबर.]] The [[सिद्धान्तन शामस्था षा समिनिच Para 1. &|सिद्धान्तसूत्र is शास्त्रस्था वा तन्निमित्त-त्वात् । पू. मी. सू.]] I. 3. 9; [[$TT explains t: irrarat # greate: T E RUT:, fier: ANTARREF fai saagaag al nacht ond. &|शबर explains यः शास्त्रस्थानां स शब्दार्थः । के शास्त्रस्थाः, शिष्टाः तेषामविच्छिन्ना स्मृतिः शब्देषु वेदेषु च । भामती on ब्रे. सू.]] III. 3. 52 relies on this for holding that the meaning assigned to a word among Aryas in India prevails over the meaning of the word among [[Andhras (e. g. of the word (a).|Āndhras (e. g. of the word राज्य).]] As regards [[to,|पीलु,]] Gaut. (I. 22) prescribes that a [[ksatriya or vaisya brahmacaria|kṣatriya or vaiśya brahmacārin]] should carry a [[staf|staff]] of the [[asvattha or pūu tree (t uhat stor),|aśvattha or pīlu tree (अश्वत्थपैलवौ शेषे),]] while [[Mano|Manu]] II. 45 [[prescribos|prescribes]] a staff of [[pīla or adumbara|pīlu or udumbara]] tree for a [[valóya brabmacı Aria.|vaiśya brahmacārin.]] The [[Amarakosa|Amarakośa]] states that pīlu [[menas|means]] a tree as well as an [[elephaat.|elephant.]] 

[[P1294]]may have two or more senses. As in several other cases Kumārila [[doos|does]] not like [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] explanation and proposes two other explanations, viz. one being that the sūtras relate to the word '[[pllu|pīlu]]' used in the sense of a tree and _mlecchas_ employ it in the sense of elephant. The meaning of the word in [[smstis|smṛtis]] is a tree and that should be followed. Here 'Śāstrasthāḥ' means 'the sense given to the word in the Śāstra i. e. [[smsti'|smṛti']]. Another meaning he sees into those [[sutras|sūtras]] is the comparative strength of smṛti and _ācāra_. This [[adhikarana|adhikaraṇa]] is relied upon by Viśvarūpa on Yāj. [[(1 225)|(I. 225)]] on the question of the primary meaning of the word Śrāddha which he states is '[[pipdadana|piṇḍadāna]]' (offering balls of boiled rice to the _manes_) and [[pot|not]] feeding brāhmaṇas. The [[Parāśara-madhaviya|Parāśara-mādhavīya]] refers to [[Parāśara-gmști|Parāśara-smṛti]] (VI. 70-71) about the food cooked from rice in such quantities as measuring an [[ādhaka|āḍhaka]][^2117] or [[drona|droṇa]] and rendered unclean by being [[packed|pecked]] by crows or touched by dogs and smelt by [[8880s|asses]] and provides that the [[measuroa|measures]] [[ādhaka|āḍhaka]] and [[drona|droṇa]] were to be taken as those known from [[sāstras|śāstras]] and not those known among mlecchas. 

Another rule (PMS I. 3. 10) about words is that[^2118] words, though of [[foreigd|foreign]] origin, that are in vogue in Sanskrit, are to be understood in the [[sonse|sense]] which they have in the foreign language and one should not think of an attempt to derive them with the help of Nirukta and grammar. [[Sabara oites|Śabara cites]] four such words viz. pika (cuckoo), nema (half), tāmarasa (lotus), sata (circular wooden vessel). 

Another rule about words is that where a single substance connected with several attributes is concerned in an action laid down for being carried out, all of these attributes should be taken to refer to the same one substance (PMS III. 1. 12). The Tai. S. prescribes 'he purchases [[Souna|Soma]] with a heifer one year old and having brown eyes and reddish in colour'. Here the two words [['piógākṣi'|'piṅgākṣī']] and [['`ekahāyanl'|'ekahāyanī']] yield a good etymological meaning, are in the same case and so refer to the same substance

The [[Highline gives guidas ma saaaaa wiata i mi morala Thai Khotarare renastere.|मयूखमालिका gives सूत्रार्थ as म्लेच्छैश्चोदितमपि प्रतीयेत । आप्तं स्यादिति यावत् । प्रमाणेनार्यव्यवहारेणाविरोधात् ।]] The underlined words [[occor lo tbe|occur in the]] sūtra. 

[[P1295]]([[helfer|heifer]] in this[^2119] case). But the word [['arupaya'|'aruṇayā']] (of red colour) causes a doubt viz. whether it should be separated from the other two words in the sentence and taken in the sense of 'any reddish substance such as a piece of cloth' or whether it should be connected with the verb (he purchases), should thus be subordinate to the purchase and should be taken as referring to the one year old heifer. This last is the established conclusion. How Soma is to be purchased does not follow from any other text. Therefore, in such a case several subsidiaries can be laid down in one injunction. If 'aruṇayā' be taken as referring to any red substance such as a piece of red cloth this one sentence would have to be divided into two vidhis 'he should purchase with a red piece of cloth' and 'he should purchase with a brown-eyed (heifer) one year old'. This would be a fault called 'vākyabheda'. This nyāya is explained by the Madanapārijāta pp. 88-89 and Aparārka (p. 1030) makes use of[^2120] it in explaining the correct import of the words of the Bṛ. Up. IV. 4. 21 'tam-etam vedānuvacanena brāhmaṇā vividiṣanti' by saying that when the highest object in view is one but the subordinate elements are different these latter are to be lumped together. 

Another rule about words is evolved by what is called 'niṣādasthapatinyāya' (PMS VI. 1. 51-52).[^2121] It is stated that the _iṣṭi_ in which an offering of boiled rice is made to Rudra is

III. 1. 12; ज्योतिष्टोमे क्रयं [[पकृत्य|प्रकृत्य]] श्रूयते । अरुणया [[पिडाक्ष्यैकदायन्या|पिङ्गाक्ष्यैकहायन्या]] सोमं क्रीणाति । इति । तत्र [[सन्देशः। फिमवणिमा कुल्ने|सन्देहः । किमवरुणिमा कृत्स्ने]] प्रकरणे [[निविशवोत ये एवैकहायन्यामिति। सपर.|निवेश्येतोत-क्रये एवैकहायन्यामिति । शबर.]] The sentence अरुणया...क्रीणाति is तै. सं. [[छ.|सू.]] II. 2. 6 states [['माक्षे कर्माण नानेको विधातुं शक्यते गुणः। अमाले तु विधीयन्ते बहवोप्येकयत्नतः' P.|'प्राप्ते कर्मणि नानेको विधातुं शक्यते गुणः । अप्राप्ते तु विधीयन्ते बहवोप्येकयत्नतः ॥' p.]] 485 quoted in [[भी. न्या. प्र.|मी. न्या. प्र.]] [[p.39|p. 39]] (Abhyankar's ed.). For example, [[प्राद्ध|श्राद्ध]] is laid [[dowa asa विधि but it|down as a विधि but if]] one wants to lay [[dowa|down]] some matters about [[माद्ध separate विधि|श्राद्ध separate विधि]] would be required for each matter, such as [[गयायां श्राद्धं दद्याद, कुतपे श्राद्धं वयाद.|गयायां श्राद्धं दद्यात्, कुतपे श्राद्धं दद्यात्.]] But where there is already no [[vidbi|vidhi]] laying down a [[guga (subsidiary mattor )|guṇa (subsidiary matter)]] there can be one main vidhi embodying several [[ganas,|guṇas,]] as laid down in [[P.M.S.|P. M. S.]] I. 4. 9.

I. 3. 15;]] [[पापविनेतु|प्रायश्चित्ते तु]] यथा स्थपतीष्टौ [[मन्त्रादिरहितोऽपि साक्षात्या|मन्त्रादिरहितोऽपि साक्षाच्छ्रुत्या]] निषादः [[कर्वतया रहीतत्वथा पखा randy i set on n.|कर्तृतया गृहीतस्तथा शूद्रः प्रायश्चित्तेषु । अपरार्क on या.]] III. 262 p. 1090. In the [[Alt. Br. 37. 7 Digadas, salagas aod evil-doers are spoken of as robbiog|Ait. Br. 37. 7 niṣādas, selagas and evil-doers are spoken of as robbing]] a rich man and [[runnlag Away|running away]] with the booty. 

[[P1296]]one that should be performed for [[niṣada-sthapati. A niṣada|niṣāda-sthapati. A niṣāda]] is a person born of a brāhmaṇa father and śūdra mother [[(vido|(vide]] Manu X. 8). He does not [[balong|belong]] to one of the three higher [[Varnas.|varṇas.]] Sthapati means 'chief or leader'. The question whether the compound word means 'a [[niṣada|niṣāda]] who is a chief (that is a karmadhāraya compound) or whether it means 'the ruler of [[niṣadas'|niṣādas']] who may not necessarily be a niṣāda himself but may be a [[ksatriya|kṣatriya]] (i. e. whether one should take the word as [[' ṣaṣthi tatpuruṣa'|'ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa']] as ('niṣādānāṁ sthapatiḥ')). The conclusion is that a [[karmadharaya|karmadhāraya]] is more powerful than a tatpuruṣa, since in the former both the words may be directly connected with [[tho|the]] verb [[(nisādaścāsau sthapatiśoa, tam yājayet)|(niṣādaścāsau sthapatiśca, tam yājayet)]]. The V. M. makes use of this 'maxim'. The [[Saunaka-smrti authorizes gūdras|Śaunaka-smṛti authorizes śūdras]] to adopt [[&|a]] son, yet some writers like Rudradhara, author of [[Suddhiviveka|Śuddhividveka]], held that adoption required a homa with mantras and as a [[sūdra|śūdra]] could not repeat vedic mantras he could not adopt. To this V. M. replies that his power to adopt a son being established by [[Saunaka's smrti|Śaunaka's smṛti]] all that is required is that he may get the homa performed through a brāhmaṇa. The [[Bhāmati on Saṅkarā's bhāsya or V, S.1. 3. 15|Bhāmatī on Śaṅkarā's bhāṣya on V. S. I. 3. 15]], where the meaning of the word 'brahmaloka' in [[Chandogya-Up. VIII.3.2|Chāndogya-Up. VIII. 3. 2]] is in question, says that the [[nisādasthapatinyaya|niṣādasthapatinyāya]] applies and so 'brahmaloka' means 'brahma as the goal' and not 'the world of [[brahma.|brahma.']] Manu XI. 54 enumerates five Mahāpātakas (deadly sins), [[ona|one]] of which is [['guryanganāgama'|'gurvaṅganāgama']] (sexual intercourse with [[gurtanganā)|gurvaṅganā)]]. Commentators have differed about the meaning of this word, some like Bhavadeva in [[Prayasoittaprakarana|Prāyaścittaprakaraṇa]] stating that, following niṣādasthapatinyāya, the word must be dissolved as a [[Karmadharaya|Karmadhāraya]] compound [[(guruh or gurvi odgau anganā ca)|(guruḥ or gurvī cāsāv-aṅganā ca)]], the meaning being one's own mother, while many others dissolve it as a tatpuruṣa compound meaning 'guror or [[gurūpām anganā'|gurūṇāṁ aṅganā']] (which would then include a step-mother, an elder brother's wife and teacher's wife &c.). Vide H. of Dh. vol. III. pp. 23–25 for a discussion of this. 

Prabhākara held that no word was significant in isolation, but that words became significant when joined together in a 

[[P1297]]sentence. Therefore, he and his followers were called [['anvita bhidhānayādinah'|'anvitābhidhānavādinaḥ']] as opposed to Kumārila and his followers who [[beld|held]] that words have different [[SODSON|senses]] of their own and then they combine in a sentence and yield a further sense of the sentence. These latter were called [['abhihitanvayavadinah::|'abhihitānvayavādinaḥ'.]] Vide notes to [[Sābityadarpaṇa|Sāhityadarpaṇa]] (I., II., X) pp. 86–88 for [[explana tions|explanations]] of these two terms by the present writer (ed. of 1956). 

Let us now turn to the interpretation of _vākya_ (sentence). The [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] and the Sāmaveda are metrical and therefore there is generally no difficulty in arriving at what constitutes in them one syntactical unit (vākya). But much of the [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] Yajurveda is in prose. Therefore, PMS (II. 1. 46) defines[^2122] what makes one vākya by stating that when a number of words serve a single purpose, but if one or a few of those words are separated from the rest, the latter words are incomplete in effecting a purpose and stand in need of the words separated, then all these words make one vākya. An example is the mantra 'I offer thee, that art dear to Agni, at the command of God Savitr with the arms of [[Asvins|Aśvins]], with the hands of Pūṣan'. This is one sentence, the purpose of which is nirvāpa (offering). For other definitions of vākya, vide the author's notes on [[Sahityadarpana|Sāhityadarpaṇa]] (II. 1) p. 34. For constituting words into a sentence with an understandable meaning expectancy[^2123] ([[(akanksa)|(ākāṅkṣā)]], compatibility ([[(yogyata)|(yogyatā)]] and proximity (sannidhi) are required, particularly the first. For example, [[Saṅkarācārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] on V. S. I. 4. 3 says that without [[ākāākṣā|ākāṅkṣā]] there is no cognition 

[^2122]: [[अर्थेकवादेकं वाक्यं साकाङ्क्षं चेद्विमागे स्यात्। पू.मी.II.|अर्थैकत्वादेकं वाक्यं साकाङ्क्षं चेद्विभागे स्यात् । पू. मी. सू. I. 4. 3.]] 

[[P1298]]that the words form a vākya. The word _ekavākyatā_ occurs in V. S. III. 4. 24 and conveys that there is a twofold [[akankss vir|ākāṅkṣā viz.]] grammatical as well as psychological. It is the listener or [[roader|reader]] who has, on hearing or reading a word, a desire to know another idea or word to get a complete [[senge.|sense.]] When [[soveral|several]] sentences, each conveying its own sense as far as it goes, are brought together having regard to the fact that one of them may be principal and the others auxiliary, they form one [[syntactioal|syntactical]] whole. This shows that sentences are of two kinds, _vākyas_ and _mahāvākyas_, as the [[Sabityadarpaṇa|Sāhityadarpaṇa]] puts it. 

It follows from the definition and the bhāṣya thereon that, in order to constitute a vākya, three conditions are [[nocessary|necessary]] viz. (1) there must be a number of words uttered or put down together (_padasamūha_), (2) the words must have an expectancy for each other (i. e. if a word in the collection is dropped no complete sense can be had), (3) all the words must [[sorve|serve]] one purpose or convey together one meaning (_arthaikatva_, [[800,|acc.]] to another view). It is not absolutely necessary that the words in order to form a vākya must be in close proximity. Even if some words intervene there may be a [[sentenco,|sentence,]] provided there [[isaksiksa|is ākāṅkṣā]] between the words. [[Sabara|Śabara]] on PMS IV. 3. 11 illustrates this well as stated in the note.[^2124] In the [[verso|verse]] quoted 'paśyasi' is to be connected with 'jaradgavam', though several other words intervene. Though there is proximity (sannidhi) between '[[pinaksi|pinakṣi]]' and 'jaradgavam' they do not form a sentence as there is no capacity as also [[akānkṣā between tbe|ākāṅkṣā between the]] two. The upshot is that _sannidhi_ (proximity) of words is generally required to form a sentence, though not necessarily required in every case. 

The different parts of a mantra text, meant to serve different purposes, may be regarded as distinct sentences. For example, the mantra in Tai. Br.[^2125] (III. 7. 5) 'I make an agreeable

[[P1299]][[Beat|seat]] (or abode) for thee (O cake [[1),|!),]] I prepare it as a very happy one with a stream of clarified butter; do sit thereon with pleased mind, be established in ambrosia, O sacrificial essence of rice'. [[Thego|These]] are two sentences, the first part being concerned with making a seat, the latter part being concerned with depositing the [[purodāsa|puroḍāśa]] on the seat. Similarly, in the passage 'I ([[out|cut]]) thee (O palāśa branch) for food, I (wash or rub) thee for vigour' there are two different sentences, that are independent of each other, as the Śat. Br. employs [[those|these]] with the words 'he cuts the [[palaba|palāśa]][^2126] branch with the words [['for food (I out) thee'|'for food (I cut) thee']], he washes it with the words 'for vigour thee'. The same rule applies to the ten different clauses [[ayuryajiona|'āyuryajñena]] kalpatām, prāṇo yajñena kalpatām' in Tai. S. I. 7. 9. [[%.|2.]] They are all different [[sentonces,|sentences.]][^2127] 

The doctrine of Vākyabheda plays a very important part in [[Mimārsā|Mīmāṁsā]] as well as in Dharmaśāstra. Vākyabheda literally means 'split of sentences'. When the sentences are equally independent and one sentence does not require or expect words from the other sentence to complete it, they should be treated as separate sentences. This is one sense of 'Vākyabheda.' Another and more frequent sense of Vākyabheda is as follows: The underlying principle of vākyabheda is that one and the same text cannot be construed as laying down two separate vidhis (prescriptions) or that when a certain matter has already been prescribed and then several auxiliary matters are to be prescribed, prescribing[^2128] all the auxiliaries in one sentence would

All this is based on [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] words [['कर्मचोवना पा आमेयावयः स्युः। कुतः । गुणानां युगपच्छासनात् । एकेनैव पाक्येनावानेको शुणो विधातुमिप्यते भवता । न च शब्दान्तरेण चोदिते कर्मण्यनेको गुणा परस्परसम्बन्ध era perea raul|'कर्मचोदना वा आग्नेयादयः स्युः । कुतः । गुणानां युगपच्छासनात् । एकेनैव वाक्येनानेको गुणो विधातुमिष्यते भवता । न च शब्दान्तरेण चोदिते कर्मण्यनेको गुणः परस्परसम्बन्धे चासति शक्यते विधातुम् ।]] (on II. 2. 6. pp. 485-486). Vide also [[4 ON|शबर on]] p. 593 [[OR|on]] II. 3. 4. The [[tea|मदनपारिजात]] (on p. 88) has an elaborate discussion upon the proper tithi and time for [[apakarma|upākarma]] in which it echoes the very words of the [[14.|तन्त्रवा.]] quoted at the beginning of this note, when [[H. 9.|मद. पा.]] states [['aut दुपरिधरणीयो वाक्यमेवः माक्षे कर्मण्यनेकगुणविधानाद!|'तथा च दुष्परिहरणीयो वाक्यभेदः प्राप्ते कर्मण्यनेकगुणविधानात्'.]] 

[[P1300]]give rise to the fault of vākyabheda (syntactical split of sentence). In Tai. S. there is the following passage 'the [[sacri ficial|sacrificial]] post is to be of udumbara tree, the udumbara (tree) is vigour, cattle is vigour; he obtains for him (the sacrificer) by means of vigour [[urj|ūrj]] (i. e. by udumbara post) vigour (i. e. cattle) for the attainment of vigour'. This is one syntactical whole (vākya). If it be said that there is a [[vidbi|vidhi]] prescribing the use of udumbara post in a sacrifice and that there is also another vidhi about the fruit viz. securing vigour (i. e. cattle); this would give rise to vākyabheda. Therefore there are no two vidhis in the [[sontence|sentence]] but only a vidhi and an [[arthavada|arthavāda]] (praise).[^2129] [[Saṅkarācārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] on V. S. III. 3. 57 remarks '[[ekam hidam vākyam Vaiśvānaravidyaviṣayam paurvāparyālocanāt pratigate ... eka vākyatāvagatau satyam vakyabheda-kalpanasyānyāyyatvāt.|ekaṁ hīdaṁ vākyaṁ Vaiśvānaravidyāviṣayaṁ paurvāparyālocanāt pratīyate ... eka-vākyatāvagatau satyāṁ vākyabheda-kalpanāsyānyāyyatvāt.]]' This is said with reference to the first aspect of the idea of Vākyabheda. 

The basic idea underlying the latter sense of vākyabheda is this. If an act or a substance or a secondary matter has already been the subject of a vidhi and if concerning the act (or substances &c.) a number of other matters (actions, [[subs tances|substances]] &c.) are enjoined in one sentence, there would be vākyabheda (i. e. there would have to be a separate vidhi for each of the other matters relating to what has already been the subject of a vidhi). On the other hand, if an action, substance or a [[guna|guṇa]] is enjoined for the first time along with several secondary matters in one sentence, there would be no fault of vākyabheda i. e. in a single vākya however long or containing many matters, if there is a single vidhi there is no fault. If, in the passage quoted on p. 1258 above[^2130] 'one [[desir ing|desiring]] prosperity should offer in sacrifice a white animal to Vāyu', one were to hold that first there is to be a vidhi about prosperity as the fruit or reward, there would be two vidhis and so the fault of vākyabheda, but if one holds that the vidhi is only concerned with offering a white animal and what follows '[[vāyurvai kṣepiṣ. tha...... bhūtim|vāyurvai kṣepiṣṭhā......bhūtiṁ]] gamayati' is merely an arthavāda (praise of the preceding vidhi), there is no vākyabheda. The fault of vākya-

[[P1301]]bheda arises when more vidhis than one are held to occur in the same vākya.[^2131]

A few examples must be cited to illustrate the principle of [[vālyabheda|vākyabheda]]. A [[sinple|simple]] example is 'graham [[sammārṣti|sammārṣṭi]]'. If one were to interpret this as meaning '[[ho|he]] is to clean the cup' and also as meaning that only a single cup is to be cleaned there would be vākyabheda. Therefore, it was decided that the singular number in 'graham' is not to be insisted upon and cleaning of all cups was to be resorted to; otherwise there would be two vidhis viz. 'graham [[sammrjyāt|sammṛjyāt]]' and '[[okam-eva|ekam-eva]] [[sammṭiyāt|sammṛjyāt]]'. [[Sabara|Śabara]] quotes (on PMS [[I.3.3|I. 3. 3]]) a śruti 'one who has a son and whose hair is dark should consecrate the vedic fires'. Agnyādhāna is laid down by [[sruti|śruti]] texts such as Tai. Br. I. 1. [[2.6|2. 6]], [[Sat.|Śat.]] II. 1. [[%.|2.]] Therefore, the above text only lays down certain subsidiary matters about it. A man may have dark hair but no son or he may have a son when his hair is turning grey. Therefore, if that sentence is meant to prescribe both attributes (having a son and also having dark hair), there would be two distinct vidhis in the same vākya, that is, there would be the fault of [[vāky& bneda|vākyabheda]], which has to be avoided. Therefore, that sentence must be understood as indicating a certain age viz. he must not be [[&|a]] mere boy (at the time of agnyādhāna) but of an age to bear [[&|a]] son, nor very old (when hair turns [[groy|grey]]). [[i. e,|i. e.]] he must be neither too young nor too old at the time of [[agayādbāna|agnyādhāna]]. A lakṣaṇā as regards the words '[[jātaputrah|jātaputraḥ]]' and '[[kṣṣpakeśaḥ|kṛṣṇakeśaḥ]]' is resorted to and lakṣaṇā is only a fault as to a word, while [[vākyabbeda|vākyabheda]] is a fault of a sentence and therefore [[laksanā|lakṣaṇā]] should be preferred to vākyabheda. The V. M. quotes (p. 115) the [[verge|verse]] of Manu[^2132]

[^2131]: [[est qui gurean Fashi 7 ama 974 fred i en fertat i que utila 4. p. 551 on II. 2 26.|बहवोऽपि ह्यर्था युगपदेकेन सम्बध्यन्ते । न च तावता वाक्यं भिद्यते । अनेकविधितो हि वाक्यभेद उक्तः । तन्त्रवा. p. 551 on II. 2. 26.]]

[^2132]: This text of Manu has been a subject of several conflicting judicial decisions, which are now of no use since there is now a law governing Hindu Adoptions viz. the Hindu Adoptions and Maintenance Act 78 of 1956, by section 12 proviso b of which the decisions in 40 Bom. 429 and in some later cases have been set aside. Those interested may read the present author's notes in his translation of the [[V.M,|V. M.,]] pp. 121-123 and the footnotes and his paper ([[Brief sketch of the Purvabimansa system pp. 37-38|'Brief sketch of the Pūrvamīmāṁsā system' pp. 37–38]] (ABORI, vol. VI. pp. 1-40) and also H. of Dh. vol. III. [[pp. 690 697|pp. 690-697]]. In [[Dattatreya V. Govind|Dattatreya v. Govind]] [[I.L.R|I. L. R.]] 40 Bom. 429 (where a person, in whom property had already become vested as the sole surviving male in the family, was given in adoption into another family) it was held, relying [[op Madu|on Manu]] IX. 142, that [[og|on]] adoption that person lost all rights to the property of the natural family that had become vested in [[bin|him]] before adoption. That decision holds that Manu laid down two propositions, viz. (1) a man forfeits by adoption into another family the property that once belonged to the family and that became vested in him before adoption, (2) A person given in adoption into another family does not, after the adoption, take any property of the family to which he belonged [[bat|but]] whose connection with the family ceased after adoption. These are two distinct propositions and to hold that [[Mano's|Manu's]] text is capable of these two propositions is liable to the fault of [[Vākyabheda|vākyabheda]]. The V. M. [[(p. 115 )|(p. 115)]] further relies on the construction of another vedic text 'He fixes half of the yūpa inside the vedi and half outside it.' This is interpreted by PMS III. 7. 13-14, [[Sabara|Śabara]] and the Tantravārtika as merely indicating a certain region for the fixing of the [[yāpa|yūpa]] and that the words are not to be taken literally, because in that case there would be [[Vākyabbeda|Vākyabheda]]. The [[guyer briags|Tupṭīkā brings]] out the meaning clearly. [['समस्तस्य यूपस्थान्तर्बहिर्वा माने माने अर्थमन्तदि-इति नियम्पते। द्वितीयस्थार्थस्य पुनरप्पा नियमप्रसको-अर्धे पहिदि-इत्यपि विधातव्यम् । ततश्चानेकार्थवादास्यभेदो मिनोस्यालया तानुषङ्गलेशव प्रसज्यते । अतश्चेशमबधारणमर्धमन्तरवेयेव न स्वर्धमेवान्तदीति । वथा सति चार्धान्तरमनियतत्वात् कदाचिदन्तरपि प्रामोतीति न पहिवः सिद्धचतीति। ... तस्मादमाम्या विशिष्ट एको देशोऽभिधीयत इत्येवमवाक्यमेवः।|'समस्तस्य यूपस्यान्तर्बहिर्वा माने प्राप्ते-अर्धमन्तर्वेदि-इति नियम्यते । द्वितीयस्यार्धस्य पुनरप्यनियमप्रसक्तौ - अर्धं बहिर्वेदि - इत्यपि विधातव्यम् । ततश्चानेकार्थत्वाद्वाक्यभेदो मिनोत्यारुप्यात्तावुपद्रवेश्व प्रसज्यते । अतश्चेदशमवधारणमर्धमन्तरवेद्येव न त्वर्धमेवान्तर्वेदीति । तथा सति चार्धन्तरमनियतत्वात् कदाचिदन्तरपि प्राप्नोतीति न बहिर्भावः सिद्ध्यतीति । ... तस्मादुभाभ्यां विशिष्ट एको देशोऽभिधीयत इत्येवमवाक्यभेदः ।']]

[[P1302]]IX. 142 'the son given shall not have (share) the family name (gotra) and the wealth (riktha) of his natural father; the [[pinda|piṇḍa]] (cake offered to deceased ancestors) follows the family name and the wealth; of him who gives (his son in adoption) the [[svadha|svadhā]] (obsequies) cease (so far as that son is concerned)'. After citing the above [[smrti|smṛti]] passage 'one having a [[gon|son]]' and another [[text'|text]] for the position of the sacrificial post (vide PMS III. 7. 13-14) with reference to the altar, the V. M. holds that one should not merely emphasize the words gotra, riktha, [[pinda|piṇḍa]] and [[svadha|svadhā]] used by Manu and take them literally but should hold that the [[verge|verse]] contains a lakṣaṇā (indicated meaning[[ );|);]] it indicates the cessation of all those consequences that are due to connection with [[tbe|the]] piṇḍa in the case of the natural father and Manu says nothing about the property taken by a son before he is given in adoption into another family.

Another illustration of vākyabheda can be cited from the law of re-union. The Mit., [[Dayabhāga|Dāyabhāga]] and Sm. [[O.|C.]] (on [[Vyavahtra|Vyavahāra]] [[p. 302 )|p. 302)]] quote [[& text 2133|a text]][^2133] of Bṛhaspati 'that man who being once separated from his father, brother, or paternal uncle again dwells

[^2133]: [[विभक्तं धनं पुनर्मिश्रीकृत संसष्टं तवस्यास्तीति संसष्टी। संशष्टत्वं च न येन केनापि किन्तु पित्रा प्रात्रा पितृष्येण का। यथावृहस्पतिः। विभको यः पुनः पित्रा मात्रा बेकत्र संस्थितः। पितृग्येणाध वा मीत्या सतसंसष्ट उच्यते । मिता. on या. II 138; दायभान chap. 12 quotes हस्पति and remarks 'परिगणितम्यतिरिक्तदु संसर्गहतो विशेषो aracufig: fopood Fer"|विभक्तं धनं पुनर्मिश्रीकृतं संसृष्टं तदस्यास्तीति संसृष्टी । संसृष्टत्वं च न येन केनापि किन्तु पित्रा भ्रात्रा पितृव्येण वा । यथाह बृहस्पतिः । विभक्तो यः पुनः पित्रा भ्रात्रा बैकत्र संस्थितः । पितृव्येणाथ वा प्रीत्या स तत्संसृष्ट उच्यते ॥ मिता. on या. II 138; दायभाग chap. 12 quotes बृहस्पति and remarks 'परिगणितव्यतिरिक्तेषु संसर्गकृतो विशेषो नादरणीयः परिगणनानर्थक्यात्' ।]]

[[P1303]]through affection with them (or any of them) is said to be re-united with them (or [[him.|him)'.]] According to the Mit., [[&|a]] reunion is possible only with father, brother and paternal uncle and with no one else, since no one else is mentioned in the text of [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]]. The V. M. does not approve of this limitation and says that a re-union is possible with all or any of those that participated in the partition and that the three persons, father, brother and uncle are mentioned only by way of illustration (i. e. there is [[lalṣanā|lakṣaṇā]]). A man may separate not only from the three named persons but also from his paternal grand-father, the grandson of his brother and the son of his paternal uncle and [[Boveral|several]] other persons and therefore the construction of the text of [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]] by the [[Mit,|Mit.]] is liable to the fault of vākyabheda, since on that interpretation there would be two separate [[proposi tions|propositions]] viz. (1) that man is to be called [[ro-united|re-united]] who having become separate again stays together with the one from whom he separated, (2) one can re-unite only with the father, brother, or paternal uncle. Thus there would be two distinct prescriptions in one sentence. Therefore, one should have [[recourge|recourse]] to [[laksanā|lakṣaṇā]] viz. that the three named [[porsons|persons]] indicate a class of persons from whom one may have [[soparated|separated]] but with whom he then stayed together. The [[Viramitrodaya|Vīramitrodaya]] (on Vyavahāra), V. R. (pp. 605–67), V. C. p. 533 hold the same view as that of the V. M.[^2134]

The [[Smrtioan drikā|Smṛticandrikā]] provides that after the death of a man when the sons divide, the mother is entitled to a share equal to that of each son if the estate to be divided is not very large, but if the estate be very large she is to get as much only as would be necessary for her maintenance (although Yāj. II. 123 and others employ the words 'samam [[ansam|aṁśam]]'). The Madanaratna on Vyavahāra criticizes this as a bad provision on the ground that it is vitiated by [[Vakyabheda|Vākyabheda]], since there would be two different propositions (vidhis) about the meaning to be attached to the

[^2134]: [[अन पितृभ्रातृपितृव्यैरेव सह संसृष्टता नान्येन बचनेनुपादानाविति मिताक्षराविषु। विभागकतसामान्याधिकरण्येनैव सेति युक्तम् । पित्रादिपदानि तु विभागकर्तृमात्रोपलक्षकाणि। Br i a ha afectatlari 37779T Tiranci , A. . 146. This text ( Hadele etc. ) is discussed in P.M.S. III. 7. 13-14 (Her Torea: 1 ETATS Arsipanerard) and the conclusion is that this text is not to be taken literally (since in that case there would be two propositions and the fault of Vakyabheda) bot as merely indicating the region where the sacrificial post is to be planted. Vide the author's notes in translation of V, M. P. 191.|अत्र पितृभ्रातृपितृव्यैरेव सह संसृष्टता नान्येन वचनेनुपादानादिति मिताक्षराविदुः। विभागकर्तृसामान्याधिकरण्येनैव सेति युक्तम् । पित्रादिपदानि तु विभागकर्तृमात्रोपलक्षणानि । अर्धमन्तर्वेदि मिनोत्यर्धं बहिर्वेदीतिवत् । अन्यथा वाक्यभेदात् । व्य. म. p. 146. This text (अर्धमन्तर्वेदि etc.) is discussed in P.M.S. III. 7. 13-14 (तथा यूपस्य वेदिः । देशमात्रं वाऽशिष्येणैकवाक्यत्वात्) and the conclusion is that this text is not to be taken literally (since in that case there would be two propositions and the fault of Vākyabheda) but as merely indicating the region where the sacrificial post is to be planted. Vide the author's notes in translation of V. M. p. 191.]]

[[P1304]]words 'samam [[amsam|aṁśam]]' (equal share) according as the estate to be divided is large or [[small. 135|small.]][^2135]

There is another principle about sentences called '[[anuṣanga|anuṣaṅga]]' (elliptical extension) or the extension of a word, phrase or clause from a sentence to another or other sentences, provided all those sentences are of the same type or form. This is one type of [[anuṣanga|anuṣaṅga]]. Another type is where each of two or more sentences appears to be complete by itself, but there are certain words of a subordinate character in the last sentence of a passage which have to be taken up or understood in the preceding [[sontences|sentences]]. This latter is also called [[anukar sa|anukarṣa]]. In the three [[upasads|Upasads]] of [[Jyotistoma|Jyotiṣṭoma]], the first[^2136] is in honour of Agni in which the mantra is '[[yā to Agne ayāśayā tanūr-varṣiṣthā gavharesthogram yaco apāvadhim tvegam vaco apāvadhim svāhā|yā te Agne ayāśayā tanūr-varṣiṣṭhā gahvareṣṭhogram vaco apāvadhīṁ tveṣaṁ vaco apāvadhīṁ svāhā]]'; the other two Upasads have the two mantras '[[yā te Agne rajasaya|yā te Agne rajāśayā]]' and '[[ya te Agne harasaya|yā te Agne harāśayā]]' which are incomplete and in need of complementary words to complete the sentences. The conclusion is that the words '[[Varsistha|Varṣiṣṭhā]] ... [[SVaha|svāhā]]' are to be [[sup. plied|supplied]] from the first sentence [[apd|and]] not other words from the popular language that one may choose. Another passage of the [[Tai,|Tai.]][^2137] [[8.|S.]] is '[[cit-patistva punatu, vakpatis-tvā punatu, devas-tva savita punatvacchidrena pavitrena vasoh sāryasya rasmibhih|cit-patistvā punātu, vākpatis-tvā punātu, devas-tvā savitā punatvacchidreṇa pavitreṇa vasoḥ sūryasya raśmibhiḥ]]'. Here the first two clauses appear to be prima facie complete, but when we look to the last clause where the word 'punātu' is [[parti cularised|particularised]] by certain other words, we at once feel that the first two clauses also should be connected with the words '[[acchidroṇa... raśmjbhiḥ|acchidreṇa... raśmibhiḥ]]' and that then they would be quite complete.

Vide स्मृतिच. II. p. 268.]]

II. 1.48 says अथेह कथं भवितव्यम, पत्र निराकांक्षाणां संनिधौ परिपरणसमर्थ श्यते। यथा चित्पति... रश्मिभिरिति । ... एकश्वासो पुनातुशब्दः पुनः पुनरुचरितः । तेनावगच्छामो यत्र पुनातु शब्दः प्रयुक्तस्तत्र तेनैकवाक्यत्वादच्छिद्रेणेत्ययमपि प्रयोक्तग्यः। ...सच पुनातुशब्दोऽ. छिदेणेत्यनेन विशिष्टः । तेन पुनातुशब्देन सानुषण नियोगतः सर्वे निराकांक्षीकर्तव्याः ।|चित्पतिस्त्वा ... रश्मिभिः । तै. सं. I. 2. 1. 2; शबर on PMS. II. 1. 48 says अथेह कथं भवितव्यम्, यत्र निराकाङ्क्षाणां संनिधौ परिपूरणसमर्थः श्रूयते । यथा चित्पति ... रश्मिभिरिति । ... एकश्चासो पुनातुशब्दः पुनः पुनरुच्चरितः । तेनावगच्छामो यत्र पुनातुशब्दः प्रयुक्तस्तत्र तेनैकवाक्यत्वादाच्छिद्रेणेत्ययमपि प्रयोक्तव्यः । ... स च पुनातुशब्दोऽच्छिद्रेणरेत्यनेन विशिष्टः । तेन पुनातुशब्देन सानुषङ्गेण नियोगतः सर्वे निराकाङ्क्षीकर्तव्याः ।]]

[[P1305]]The V. M. has a long disquisition on the principle of [[anusanga|anuṣaṅga]] applied by the Mit. and Madanaratna to the succession to the wealth of one who died re-united. Yāj. II. [[135-136|135–136]] lay down the order of succession to the wealth of a person dying sonless. [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] II. 137 deals with succession to the property of a forest hermit, an ascetic or a perpetual Vedic student. The [[Mit then bolds|Mit. then holds]] that Yāj. [[II, 138 samststinastu saṁspsti'|II. 138 'saṁsṛṣṭinastu saṁsṛṣṭī']] is to be construed as an exception to Yāj. II. [[135-136|135–136]] and understands that the words 'of one dying without having a son' (grandson or great-grandson) are to be supplied before Yāj. [[II, 138|II. 138]] from II. 136 (i. e. there is to be [[anusanga|anuṣaṅga]] of the words 'svaryātasyāputrasya'). The V. M. does not approve of this and states that there is no rational ground for applying the principle of [[anusanga|anuṣaṅga]] and therefore the order of succession to the property left by one re-united propounded by V. M. becomes different from that of the Mit. The whole discussion is rather abstruse and lengthy and the present writer feels that the space required for exposition should be saved. Those interested may consult the exhaustive notes (pp. [[265-275|265–275]]) in the edition of the [[Vyavahāramayukba|Vyavahāramayūkha]] (Poona, 1926) on the passages of the V. M. beginning with the one in the note [[below. 2138|below.]][^2138]

This principle of [[anuṣanga|anuṣaṅga]] cannot apply where words of a different type intervene between the several sentences. For example, when the animal to be offered is killed a long passage occurs which contains the [[2139 words|words]][^2139] '[[sam te prāņo vāyunā gacchatām, sam yajatrnir-aūgāni, sam yajñapatir-āsiṣa'|sam te prāṇo vāyunā gacchathāṁ, sam yajatrvair-aṅgāni, sam yajñapatir-āśiṣā']] &c. (may your limbs be joined to the worshipful deities and may the sacrificer be united to blessing [[&o ).|&c).]] Here the first clause is

[^2138]: [[2138.|]]संसृष्टिनस्तु संसृष्टी सोदरस्य तु सोदरः। पत्नी दुहितरः (या. 2. 135-136) इत्यादिप्रपवादोऽयम् । ... यत्तु विज्ञानेश्वरप्रभृत- यगोऽपवादयोस्समानविषयत्वनियमात् पूर्वोक्त-स्वर्यातस्यापुत्रस्येत्येतत्पदानुषङ्गादेतदपि पुत्रपौत्रप्रपौत्रहीनविषयम्; । अतस्तादृश[[पुतसंसृष्ट्रिधनमसंसृष्टिसंनिहितपत्न्यादिसद्भावेऽप्यन्यः|पुतसंसृष्टिधनमसंसृष्टिसंनिहितपत्न्यादिसद्भावेऽप्यन्यः]] संसृष्ट्येव गृह्णीयादिति । तच्चिन्त्यम् । तेन विनापि गतौ सम्भवन्त्यामनुषङ्गे मानाभावात् । व्य. म. p. 147; vide मदनरत्न (on व्यवहार) p. 372 'न च संसृष्टिनस्तु संसृष्टीति याज्ञवल्क्यवाक्येऽपुत्रस्येति पदानुश्रवणात्संसृष्टिमरणमेव प्रयोजकंमिति वाच्यं, स्वर्यातस्य ह्यपुत्रस्येति वाक्योपात्तस्यापुत्रस्येति पदस्य स्वर्यातस्येत्युपदवत् संसृष्टिवाक्येऽनुषङ्गात्' ।

[^2139]: [[2139.|]]व्यवधायाच्चानुषज्येत । पू. मी. सू. II. 1. 49; [[Sabara|Śabara]] says सं ते वायुर्वातेन गच्छतां सं यजत्रैरङ्गानि सं यज्ञपतिराशिषा इति । वायुर्वातेन गच्छताम्-इत्येष सं यजत्रैरङ्गानि-इति बहुवचनान्तेन व्यवहितत्वात् सं यज्ञपतिराशिषा इत्यत्र नानुषज्यते । ... तस्माद्बहुवचनान्तरस्य परस्य च तद्व्यपेतस्य लौकिको वाक्यशेषः कर्तव्य इति । The तै. सं. I. 3. 8. 1 reads 'सं ते प्राणो वायुना'. It is probable that [[Sabara|Śabara]] quotes from memory or sets out the mantra from the Mai. [[Samhita|Saṁhitā]] [[7. 2. 15|I. 2. 15]] which reads [[संते वायुतिन,|सं ते वायुर्वातेन.]]

[[P1306]]separated from the 3rd clause by a clause in which there are two words in the plural while the first and third clauses have two words in the singular; therefore there is no [[anuṣanga|anuṣaṅga]] of the words of the first clause into the 2nd and one may employ for completing the sense of the 3rd clause some ordinary word from popular language (and not the words from the 1st clause).

Veda enjoins many acts such as performing a yāga, casting an oblation into fire, making a gift, milking a cow, melting clarified butter &c, but all are not on the same level. Some [[aro|are]] pradhāna (primary[^2140] or principal), while others are [[guṇa bbūta|guṇa-bhūta]] (subsidiary). The acts denoted by words such as Prayājas by which some substance is not embellished or not made fit or is not produced are primary, while those which produce a substance or make it fit (such as pounding in the case of grains of paddy) are subsidiary. Acts are further classified into [[Various|various]] groups such as nitya, [[naimiltıka|naimittika]] or [[kūmya|kāmya]] or as [[kratyartha|kratvartha]] and [[puruṣartha|puruṣārtha]]. These have already been explained. There are six means[^2141] by which is ascertained the difference or [[non difference|non-difference]] among acts viz. (1) [[sabdāntara|śabdāntara]] (different words, such as yajati, juhoti, dadāti, [[i. o.|i. e.]] yāga, homa, dāna are different acts); [[abhyāsa 2113|abhyāsa]][^2142] (repetition) as in 'samidho yajati, [[tanūna. pātam|tanūnapātaṁ]] yajati' [[&o.|&c.]] (Tai. [[8.|S.]] II. 6. 1. [[1-%),|1–2),]] where the word yajati is repeated five times and therefore there are five different acts prescribed; (3) [[sankhyā|saṅkhyā]] (number) as in 'he sacrifices seventeen animals to Prajāpati' (Tai. [[Br,|Br.]] I. 3. 4. 3), which are seventeen distinct acts; (4) [[guna|guṇa]] (an accessory detail [[suoh|such]] as the deity

II. 1. 1 'भावार्थाः कर्मशब्दास्तेभ्यः क्रिया प्रतीयेतैष ह्यर्थो विधीयते'।]] These six are mentioned [[io|in]] P. M. S. II. 2. 1., II. 2. 2 ([[39R|अभ्यास]]), II. 2. 21 ([[**41|संख्या]]), [[II, 2.23 (947),|II. 2. 23 (गुण),]] II. 2. 22 ([[alata or *1|नामधेय or संज्ञा]]), [[Herfor (or \#41) Ib|प्रकरण (or प्रक्रिया) in]] II. 3. 24. [[Sabara|Śabara]] mentions the six [[io|in]] one order, while the P. M. S. deals with them in a slightly different order. [[Parāśara 1. 38|Parāśara I. 38]] says that a man should be intent on six actions, viz. bath, sandhyā etc. and relies on [[sabdantara|śabdāntara]] to establish that they are six distinct acts and not one composite [[ect.|act.]] Vide [[101. 1. I. 1. 2.|परा. मा. I. 1. 2.]]

[[P1307]]or substance in such a passage as 'when [[curda|curds]][^2143] are put into heated milk it turns into [[curdlad|curdled]] solid mass āmikṣā which is offered to [[Visvedevas|Viśvedevas]] and the liquid called vājina is to be offered to [[Vajins,|Vājins,]] these two being two distinct offerings); (5) [[prakarana,|prakaraṇa]] (context). In the sentence 'one should offer agnihotra' (Tai. S. I. 5. [[9.1)|9. 1)]] there is a vidhi of the daily performance of [[Agnihotra|Agnihotra.]] In the [[Kundapāyinām-ayana|Kuṇḍapāyinām-ayana]][^2144] it is said 'he offers agnihotra for a month'. Since this last [[oocurs|occurs]] in a different context altogether (while the former occurs in the context of [[Darsa pūrṇamāsa),|Darśapūrṇamāsa),]] this sentence (from [[Kuṇdapāyinām-ayana)|Kuṇḍapāyinām-ayana)]] deals with a different act from that of the daily Agnihotra. (6) The name ([[Sanjñā)|Sañjñā)]] also differentiates acts, since they [[ocour|occur]] in utpattivākya (originative [[injunction )|injunction)]] as in the passage quoted [[jn|in]] the note below.[^2145] This technique about determining what actions are different on account of the several grounds noted above, was employed by [[Hemadri,|Hemādri,]] the [[Kalanirṇaya|Kālanirṇaya]] and the [[Nirnayasindhu|Nirṇayasindhu]] in deciding whether [[Janmāṣtamīvrata|Janmāṣṭamīvrata]] and [[Jayantivrata|Jayantīvrata]] are really one vrata or separate vratas. Vide pp. 132–133 above.

It has already been stated above (p. 1228) that there are four kinds of vidhis of which viniyogavidhi is one. This last conveys the relation between a religious act as principal ([[seṣin|śeṣin]] or [[angin)|aṅgin)]] [[and 2146|and]][^2146] its auxiliaries ([[angas).|(aṅgas).]] This is the concern of the third chapter of the P. M. S., which first defines [['$eṣa',|'śeṣa',]] states why it is called [['besa',|'śeṣa',]] how it is employed in religious acts, what are the means of determining the relation of [[sega|śeṣa]] to [[seṣin|śeṣin]] and the comparative strength of each of the means.

[[P1308]]Some examples of [[anga|aṅga]] and [[angin|aṅgin]] may have to be given here. In [[' vribin|'vrīhīn]] prokṣati' (he sprinkles rice grains with water) the sprinkling (prokṣaṇa) is an [[anga|aṅga]] (stands in [[&|a]] subsidiary relation to) of rice grains as directly shown by the objective case ([[vrihin).|(vrīhīn).]] The [[prokṣapa (is motivated by)|prokṣaṇa (is motivated by)]] has the purpose of contributing to the transcendental result (the apūrva), since what is meant is that if, without the sprinkling of water on the rice grains, a sacrifice [[(yāga )|(yāga)]] were performed, apūrva would not be brought about. To take another [[example, 2147|example,]][^2147] 'he takes hold of the bridle of the horse with (the mantra beginning with ) 'they took hold of the bridle of [[pta|ṛta]] (cosmic [[order).|order)'.]] Here the direct assertion (śruti) shown by the objective case in [['raśanām.|'raśanām']] shows that that mantra stands in a subordinate relation to (is an [[abga|aṅga]] of) the bridle of the horse, since the utterance of it when taking hold of the bridle of the horse effects a [[samskāra|saṁskāra]] in the bridle and taking hold is also an [[anga|aṅga]] of the horse bridle (which is in the objective [[case ),|case),]] just as prokṣaṇa is an [[anga|aṅga]] of [[rica|rice]] grains.

It has already been stated that [[' sasa'|'śeṣa']] means 'what subserves the purpose of another' and it is śeṣa of that other (P. M. S. III. 1. [[2. Segah|2 'Śeṣaḥ]] [[pararthatvāt')|parārthatvāt')]] and that, according to [[Badari|Bādari]] (III. 1. 3) 'to substances, properties (like reddish colour of a cow), [[samskaras 2118|saṁskāras]][^2148] (what makes a person or thing fit for being employed in a yāga or for any other purpose) the word [[soṣa|śeṣa]] is always applied, while, acc. to Jaimini (III. 1. [[4-6)|4–6)]] religious rites are [[sosa|śeṣa]] to the result or fruit (expressed or implied), the fruit is [[sesa|śeṣa]] to the agent of religious acts and an agent is [[sega|śeṣa]] to certain acts. The word [[“sosa'|'śeṣa']] is often employed by [[Dharm aśāstra worke.|Dharmaśāstra works.]] For example, the Mit. while commenting on [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] II. 118–119 states that the first half of verse 118 is śeṣa to all the [[rest 2119|rest]][^2149] [[( i. .|(i. e.]] serves the purpose of the remaining verse and [[& half).|a half).]] The result is (if the Mit. be followed) that if a gift is

The words 'imām-agṛbhṇan raśanām-ṛtasya' constitute a quarter of the mantra in Tai. S. IV. 1. 2. 1.]]

[[P1309]]acquired (by [[& coparoener|a coparcener]]) from a grateful friend who was placed under an obligation by spending family wealth, if a property is acquired by a gift from a member's father-in-law who was paid from the family property for securing the bride for that member, or if the property of the family lost to it was [[re covered|recovered]] by a member with the help of (other paternal estate), or if a member of the family learnt at the expense of the family and made gains from that learning, then those kinds of property were liable to be partitioned among all members. This view of the Mit. was not shared by several other writers and works such as the Dāyabhāga (VI. [[1.8),|1. 8),]] and Viśvarūpa. Vide H. of Dh. vol. III. [[pp. 579-580|pp. 579–580]] for further remarks.

The P. M. [[S speaks|S. speaks]] of six means that are helpful in [[deter minig,|determining,]] as regards viniyoga-vidhis, what are principal and what are auxiliary when there is a doubt or when there is a [[conflict, They|conflict. They]] are śruti (direct vedic statement or assertion), linga (indirect indication), vākya (syntactical connection or relation), [[prakarana|prakaraṇa]] (context), sthāna (place or sequence), samākhyā (sañjñā, name). When several of these come together and refer to the same subject each succeeding one is weaker than each preceding one, since each succeeding one is more remote than [[sach|each]] preceding one from the objective (viz. the viniyoga). P. M. S. III. 3. 14 is called [["balabalādhikaraña',|'balabalādhikaraṇa'.]][^2150]

An example where both śruti and [[liiga|linga]] come in conflict is [['with the Aindri verse (verge addressed to Indra) he should worship the Gārbapatya fire'.|'with the Aindrī verse (verse addressed to Indra) he should worship the Gārhapatya fire'.]] The verse addressed to Indra[^2151]

[[P1310]][[ne addresse detall (suna)" he performs |]]is '[[nivesanaḥ sangamano vagūbām...Indro na tasthau samare pathinām'(Tai.|niveśanaḥ saṅgamano vasūnām...Indro na tasthau samare pathīnām' (Tai.]] S. IV. 2. 5. 4). Here the doubt arises whether one should worship Indra as the word '[[Aindrya'|Aindryā']] indicates or whether one should worship [[Gārbapatya|Gārhapatya]] (as the passage directly asserts) with a verse addressed to Indra, or whether one should worship both or whether one should worship either Indra or Gārhapatya as one likes. The conclusion is that [[sruti|śruti]] (direct Vedic assertion) is more powerful than linga. On hearing the words [['Garhapatyam upatiṣthate'|'Gārhapatyam upatiṣṭhate']] we are directly told by the Veda about the worship of [[Garhapatya.|Gārhapatya.]] The word '[[Aindrya'|Aindryā']] being in the instrumental (as in [['dadhna juhoti'-he|'dadhnā juhoti'—he]] performs homa with curds) merely supplies a detail [[(guna) vix,| (guṇa) viz.]] that the mantra to be repeated is one addressed to Indra and there is no word that directly asserts that Indra is to be worshipped.[^2152] [[Sabara|Śabara]] on PMS [[JIL|III.]] 2. 4 explains that even [[Gārbapatya|Gārhapatya]] has some of the qualities of Indra and so metaphorically he may be called Indra ([[ as|as]] a brave man is called a lion), since Gārhapatya is [[&|a]] means of [[accomplisbing|accomplishing]] yajña like Indra or [[Gārbapatya|Gārhapatya]] may be called Indra from the root 'ind', and may be taken to [[mean's|mean 'a]] master or lord'.

The six means may each come in conflict with all the means that follow each of them. Therefore, there will be five cases of conflict of śruti with linga (which has already been [[desoribed )|described)]] or with vākya, or [[prakarapa|prakaraṇa]] or sthāna or samākhyā; there will be four cases of conflict between [[lióga|linga]] and [[vākys|vākya]] or each of the three ones [[( of the means)|(of the means)]] that follow [[vakya|vākya]] in the sūtra, [[ia|in]] the same way vākya may be in conflict with [[prakarapa|prakaraṇa]] and the other two (in all three cases), [[prakarapa|prakaraṇa]] may be in conflict with sthāna or samākhyā (i. e. two cases) and sthāna may be in conflict with samākhyā. So that there are in all fifteen cases of conflict of the six means among themselves. We need [[act|not]]

[[P1311]]take into account the conflict of one of the six means with its predecessors, since to say that linga may be in conflict with [[bruti|śruti]] is the same thing as saying that śruti may be in conflict with linga. Very large space would have to be devoted if all these fifteen possible conflicts are to be exemplified from Vedic and Dharmaśāstra texts. Therefore, the author does not essay that task.

Dharmaśāstra works often utilize this [[adhikarana|adhikaraṇa]] called [[balabala|balābala]] (PMS III. 3. 14). For example, the Par. M. quotes[^2153] [[&|a]] śruti passage that one should perform the daily evening sandhyā adoration of [[Aditya|Āditya]] (the sun) with mantras addressed to [[Varupa|Varuṇa]] and remarks that [[“ Vārupibhiḥ"|“Vāruṇībhiḥ"]] (like Aindryā) is only linga while, [[' Adityam-upsthāya'|'Ādityam-upatiṣṭhāya']] is [[sruti (|śruti (]]direct assertion) and therefore the sun is to be worshipped in the evening with mantras addressed to [[Varuna|Varuṇa]] and refers to the example [['aindrya gārhapatyam upatiṣthate'|'aindryā gārhapatyam upatiṣṭhate']] for support.

The fourth adhyāya of the PMS deals mainly with the [[subo ject|subject]] of prayojya and prayojaka and kratvartha and puruṣārtha. The latter two have been explained and illustrated above (pp. [[1232-1235|1232–1235]]). A few illustrations of the first two may be given. The Prayājas have been declared above to be [[keratvartha|kratvartha]] (p. [[1233 ).|1233).]] Therefore, kratu [[( sacrifice)|(sacrifice)]] is the prayojaka [[( motivating force )|(motivating force)]] of Prayājas. The reward (phala, [[Svarga|svarga]] or the like) is the prayojaka of yāga or [[&|a]] puruṣārtha rite. That is a prayojaka for the sake of which [[&|a]] man is led to perform something by a Vedic exhortatory passage. The sentence is [[' one|'one]] should offer the [[Darsapūrṇamāsa saori fice|Darśapūrṇamāsa sacrifice]] for securing svarga'; therefore the [[toward ( svarga and the like )|reward (svarga and the like)]] is the prayojaka of Darśapūrṇamāsa-yāga.[^2154] The injunction to mix [[ourde|curds]] with milk makes a person do so for producing [[Imikṣā|āmikṣā]] and not vājina, since this latter arises of it-
