[[P1312]]self in preparing [[amikṣi.|āmikṣā.]] Therefore, it is [[amiksa,|āmikṣā,]] being the havis in the [[Vaiśvadovayāga|Vaiśvadevayāga]] that is the prayojaka of the [[Vaiśvadovayāga|Vaiśvadevayāga]] and vājina-yāga is not the prayojaka of putting curds into [[milk 1155|milk]][^2155] (P. M. S. [[IV.1 22-24).|IV. 1. 22–24).]] The result is that, if by accident āmikṣā is destroyed, in order to secure the havis [[(āmiksā )|(āmikṣā)]] curds will have again to be put in boiled milk, but if [[zājin,|vājina,]] not being prayojaka, is destroyed, [[ourds|curds]] need not be put into boiled milk [[again,|again.]]

Examples of puruṣārtha acts have been given above [[!p. 1234)|(p. 1234)]] such as the Prājāpativrata. This chapter (in 2nd pāda) deals with several cases of pratipattikarma and arthakarma already explained [[abɔve (pp. 1231-3?).|above (pp. 1231–32).]] There are certain [[substanov8,|substances,]] certain embellishments and subsidiary actions with which some phala is associated. For example, it is said (in Tai. [[8.|S.]] III. [[7.5.2)'he|7. 5. 2) 'he]] who has juhū ladle made of parṇa [[( palāśa )|(palāśa)]] wood [[nover bears|never hears]] an evil word about himself'; 'that he applies collyrium (to his eyes), he injures the eye of his enemy' (Tai. S. VI. [[I. 1.5);|1. 1. 5);]] 'That he performs the Prayājas and Anuyājas, that is indeed the armour of the [[sacrifice:|sacrifice'.]] PMS declares that these texts about rewards associated with substances, embellishments and [[sube sidiary|subsidiary]] acts are really not [[ridhis|vidhis]] about rewards but are [[more|mere]] [[arthavadas,|arthavādas,]] because all these serve the purpose of the principal [[kratu. 2356|kratu.]][^2156]

This fourth chapter (in 3rd pāda) also decides that, [[al though|although]] no reward is expressly declared by Śruti (the Veda) for the performance of the Viśvajit sacrifice, still in the Viśvajit sacrifice [[( and|(and]] in other sacrifices where no reward is expressly [[mentioned )|mentioned)]] [[syarga|svarga]] is the reward.[^2157]

IV. 3. 15. सर्वान् means सर्वपुरुषान्. शबर explains 'सर्वे हि पुरुषाः स्वर्गकामाः । कुत एतत् । प्रीतिर्हि स्वर्गः । सर्वश्च प्रीतिं प्रार्थयते ।; मेधा. on मनु II. 2. refers to it; vide परा. मा. [[P1313]]svam [[dadati'),|dadāti').]] Jaimini devotes about [[tourteen adhikaranas iss|fourteen adhikaraṇas]][^2158] to it, viz. IV. [[3. 10-16|3. 10–16]] (three [[adhikaranas),|adhikaraṇas),]] VI. [[7.1–20|7. 1–20]] ([[nipo adhikaranas),|nine adhikaraṇas),]] [[VIL.3.6-11(one|VII. 3. 6–11 (one]] [[adhi.),x.6.13-14|adhi.), X. 6. 13–14]] (one adhi.). Some of the interesting propositions are : the performer cannot donate his own relatives (such as father or mother) but can [[denote|donate]] that of which he is real owner or master; that even the emperor cannot make a gift of the whole kingdom, as other people have rights over land and the king protects the people and has only the right to a certain share in the produce of the land; that the performer cannot donate [[borge8|horses]] as [[gruti|śruti]] expressly forbids the gift of horses in Viśvajit; that the performer can donate only that which exists as his property at the time of giving [[dakśiṇā|dakṣiṇā]] in the sacrifice and not that which may become his at a future date; that a [[sūdra|śūdra]][^2159] who waits upon the performer because it is his duty to serve (acc. to Manu) cannot be donated; a person of a higher varṇa cannot be donated; only he who has 112 cows or more wealth is entitled to perform Viśvajit.

[[___The 5th adhyaya of P.M.S.deals|The 5th adhyāya of P. M. S. deals]] with krama (the order in which the several component parts or acts that go to make up a sacrifice are to come one after another). Vidhis speak of the performance of many acts in a sacrifice and do not always lay down the order in which those acts (principal and auxiliary) are to be performed. It would not do to perform them in any order that the performer likes. For determining the order of sequence of several acts in a sacrifice one has to depend upon [[Bir means|six means]][^2160] viz. [[sruti|śruti]] (direct assertion as to order), artha

II pp. 849-50 which summarise PMS, VI.1.1-7, व्य. म. p. 92. विश्वजिति ... ददाति occurs in कोषी. बा. 25.14. मेघा remarks "कुस्नगोलकाभिप्रायमेव चादेयत्वं भूमविश्वजिति मीमांसकेरक्त 'न भूमिः स्यात्।। सर्वान पुरुषान् प्रति चक्रमणादियोग्यतयाऽविशिष्टा भूमिः स्वामीकमशक्या कथं दीयते ह्यर्थ: ।.|स्वदाने सर्वमविशेषात् (पूर्वपक्ष) । यस्य वा प्रभुः स्यादितरस्याशक्यत्वात् (सिद्धान्त) । IV. 7. 1–2; vide H. of Dh, vol. II pp. 849–50 which summarise PMS, VI. 7. 1–7, व्य. म. p. 92. विभजति ... ददाति occurs in कौषी. ब्रा. 25. 14. मेधा० remarks 'कृत्स्नगोलकाभिप्रायमेव चादेयत्वं भूमेर्विभाजिति मीमांसकैरुक्तं 'न भूमिः स्यात्' । सर्वान् पुरुषान् प्रति चङ्क्रमणप्रवियोग्यतयाऽविशिष्टा भूमिः स्वामीकर्तुमशक्या कथं दीयते इत्यर्थः ॥'.]]

[^2160]: [[इवानी क्रमनियमलक्षणमुच्यते । तस्यर्थ-पाठ-प्रवृत्ति काण्ड-मुख्यैर्वक्ष्यते । इत्यादीनां चपलापलम् । शबर on पू. मी. . v.1.1. It may be noticed that both अर्यसंग्रह and मी. न्या. म. slightly differ from Sabara in the order and names of theaix means vir, as तत्र पद्ममाणानि श्रुत्यर्थपाठस्थानमुख्यमवस्याख्यानि । तत्र कमो नाम विवातिविशेषः पौर्वापर्यरूपो था। अर्धसंग्रह p. 12 and मी. न्या. म. p. 1735|इहेदानीं क्रमनियमलक्षणमुच्यते । तच्छ्रुत्यर्थ-पाठ-प्रवृत्ति-काण्ड-मुख्यैर्वक्ष्यते । श्रुत्यादीनां च बलाबलम् । शबर on पू. मी. सू. V. 1. 1. It may be noticed that both अर्थसंग्रह and मी. न्या. प्र. slightly differ from Śabara in the order and names of the six means viz. as तत्र षट् प्रमाणानि श्रुत्यर्थपाठस्थानमुख्यप्रवृत्त्याख्यानि । तत्र क्रमो नाम विततिविशेषः पौर्वापर्यरूपो वा । अर्थसंग्रह p. 12 and मी. न्या. प्र. p. 173;]]

[[P1314]]([[purposa,|purpose,]] [[suitability ),|suitability),]] [[patha|pāṭha]] (verbal [[taxta ),|texts),]] [[pravztti|pravṛtti]] ([[commande. ment),|commencement),]] [[kāṇda|kāṇḍa]] (place in the [[texta),|texts),]] mukhya (principal).

In the Vedic passage about the [[dikṣā in & saftra,|dīkṣā in a sattra,]] the [[adbparya,|adhvaryu,]] after performing the [[diksa|dīkṣā]] (initiation rite) on the [[shapati|gṛhapati]] (the yajamāna, the performer of the sacrifice) performs [[dikṣā|dīkṣā]] on the brahmā priest, then on [[udgāt?|udgātṛ]] etc. the Vedic text directly lays down the sequence (by means of the [[tormination "ty!)|termination 'tvā')]] that the dīkṣā of brahmā priest [[cones|comes]] after the dīkṣā [[af|of]] the yajamāna. In the passage 'samidho yajati [[tanūnapatam|tanūnapātaṁ]] yajati [[&c.|&c'.]] the very order in which the sentences occur in the text (i. e. [[patha)|pāṭha)]] determines the order of the performance of the [[Beveral|several]] yāgas (PMS [[V.1.4).|V. 1. 4).]] The [[Voda|Veda]] first speaks of the [[offar ing|offering]] of Agnihotra and then of the cooking of gruel. Here the offering of Agnihotra comes first and preparation of gruel [[comas|comes]] after that. But, unless the material to be offered is ready, no Agnihotra offering can be made. Therefore, here the [[pathae krama|pāṭhakrama]] has to be given up and [[arthakrana|arthakrama]] (order as [[diotated|dictated]] by the purpose and [[guitability )|suitability)]] has to be followed [[i.e.|i. e.]] first [[gruol|gruel]] must be prepared and then Agnihotra offered.[^2161] This is an example showing that arthakrama is more powerful than [[pathakrama|pāṭhakrama]] ([[PM8|PMS]] [[V.4.1).|V. 4. 1).]] The [[Parāśarasmrti|Parāśarasmṛti]] lays down that every day one should perform [[sandhya|sandhyā]] (morning [[adoration ),|adoration),]] bath, [[jape|japa]] ([[montto ring|muttering]] of sacred [[texts ),|texts),]] homa, study of the [[Vedah,|Veda,]] worship of gods, Vaiśvadeva and feeding guests. The Par. M. says that[^2162] setting aside the [[pathakrama|pāṭhakrama]] one must follow the suitability and therefore bath comes first and sandhyā afterwards. The [[8m. C.|Sm. C.]] quotes Vṛddhamanu to the effect that a childless chaste widow should offer [[pinda|piṇḍa]] to her deceased husband and take [[hia|his]] wealth. [[Hore|Here]] it is proper to hold that she should first take his wealth and then perform his [[sraddhas.|śrāddhas.]] In the [[Vajapeya|Vājapeya]] the text says that the performer (yajamāna) has to tie to the [[sacrifoial|sacrificial]] post seventeen paśus to be offered to [[Prajapati|Prajāpati]] (Tai. Br. I. [[3.4.|3. 4.]]

II. p. 291.|सन्ध्या स्नानं जपो होमो देवतातिथिपूजनम् । आतिथ्यं वैश्वदेवं च षट्कर्माणि दिने दिने ॥ पराशरस्मृति I. 39; vide परा. मा. I. 2. 18 that says 'सन्ध्यानन्तरमनित्यत्र यवागूपार्कन्यायेन स्नानस्य प्राथम्यं व्याख्येयम् । ... 'यवाग्वाग्निहोत्रं जुहोति यवागूं च पचति' इति श्रूयते । ... यवाग्वा इति तृतीया श्रुत्या होमसाधनत्वावगमात्सति च द्रव्ये होमानिर्वृत्तेरर्थाद्यवागूपाकः पूर्वभावीति सिद्धान्तः । एवमत्रापि स्नानस्य शुद्धिहेतुत्वाच्छुद्रस्यैव सन्ध्यावन्दनाधिकारित्वात्स्नानं पूर्वभावीति द्रष्टव्यम् । वृद्धमनुः - अपुत्रा शयनं भर्तुः पालयन्ती व्रते स्थिता । पत्न्येव दद्यात्तत्पिण्डं कृत्स्नमंशं लभेत च ॥ and remarks 'उत्तरार्धं त्वर्थक्रमेण पाठक्रमबाधो द्रष्टव्यः । तत्रायमर्थः । उक्तलक्षणा पत्न्येव भर्तृशं कृत्स्नं लभेत पश्चात्पिण्डं दद्यात् । न पुनस्तत्पांसर्गा ब्रात्रादिरिति । स्मृतिच. II. p. 291.]]

[[P1315]][[2-3).|2–3).]] It is also provided that each of the 17 paśus should have several [[samskāras|saṁskāras]] performed on it such as [[proksana (consacra tion|prokṣaṇa (consecration]] by sprinkling sacred water on it), [[upakarana|upākaraṇa]] (bringing near). One may take any of the 17 animals first and begin to perform the first of the [[samskaras|saṁskāras]] on it; but having once begun with a particular animal he must perform the second and other [[samakāras|saṁskāras]] one after another on the same animal; [[1.o,|i. e.]] the order of the [[samskāras|saṁskāras]] on an animal is determined by the [[commence ment.|commencement.]] Kāṇḍa or sthāna is exemplified as [[follows:|follows :]] The [[Jyotiṣ toma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] is the model sacrifice [[(prakrti)|(prakṛti)]] of which [[Sadyaskra|Sādyaskra]] is a [[vikrti (modification ).|vikṛti (modification).]] It is prescribed by the Veda in the case of [[Sadyaskra|Sādyaskra]] that all animals should be sacrificed together at the [[savanlya|savanīya]] stage.[^2163] In [[Jyotistoma,|Jyotiṣṭoma,]] there are three animals offered [[viz.'agpisomiya'|viz. 'agniṣomīya']] in the morning, [['savanlya'|'savanīya']] in the noon [[and' anubandhya'|and 'anubandhya']] in the evening. Sādyaskra, being a [[vikyti,|vikṛti,]] all these have to be performed in it; but the particular text about it provides that all three must be sacrificed together at the savanīya stage. This [[( offering|(offering]] all three simultaneously) is [[im possible|impossible]] and therefore all that can be done is to offer (them) one after another (and not at three different times in the day); it would appear at first sight that the [[Agaisomiya|Agniṣomīya]] paśu comes first; but that is not so, since simultaneous offerings being laid down at the [[savanlya|savanīya]] stage in [[sadyaskrayāga,|sādyaskrayāga,]] the [[advaniya pasu|savanīyapaśu]] is to be first offered (and not [[Agnizomiya)|Agniṣomīya)]] and then the [[Agnizomiya|Agniṣomīya]] immediately after and then Anubandhya or [[those|these]] two may be offered in any order one likes (but immediately).

An example of sequence being determined by the mukhya [[(first or principal )|(first or principal)]] is : there is a śruti passage [['two Sarasvata, offerings|'two Sārasvata offerings]] are to be made; this is indeed [[& divine couple', 2864|a divine couple'.]][^2164]

[^2163]: [[*a: nauti care arater featu: Tardisega: laura धान कस्माश्चित्पशोरारण्य कार्यम् । द्वितीयरत पदार्थो येन क्रमेण प्रथमोहितस्तेनैव क्रमेणाः 54: sararasuarate FAUTS anechalari H. 41. 9. pp. 189–190.|अतः प्राजापत्येष्वेकं पदार्थं सर्वेष्वनुष्ठाय द्वितीयः पदार्थोऽनुष्ठेयः । तत्र प्रथमपदार्था-नुष्ठानं कस्माच्चित्पशोरारभ्य कार्यम् । द्वितीयस्तु पदार्थो येन क्रमेण प्रथमोऽनुष्ठितस्तेनैव क्रमेणा-नुष्ठेयः प्रयोगविध्यवगतस्य मिथोऽङ्गसाहित्यरूपोपपत्तये । मी. न्या. प्र. pp. 189–190.]]

[^2164]: [[सारस्वती भवत एतदै देण्यं मिथुन देण्यमेवारमै मिथुनं मन्यो दधाति पुष्टौ प्रज WETTI . II. 4. 6. 1-2. This occurs in relation to paar in which seven mubsidiary offerings are prescribed of which two are FRF. Hemat means i fremlagarsi Fragaanyat perats. The qoft. & is gel mat manusia (V.1. 14 07.) F. II. 135 mentions as heirs to a man dying soplosa, bis wife, daughter and parents (foret). Sappose & man dies leaving his father and mother as nearest relatives, who should succeed, vit. whether mother should be preferred to father or vios versa or whether both should take the estate equally, The FATTET prefers the mother to the father, the Sm, C, refers to this lastance of pictata and there being|सारस्वतौ भवत एतद्वै दैव्यं मिथुनं दैव्यमेवास्मै मिथुनं मध्यतो दधाति पुष्ट्यै प्रजननाय । तै. सं. II. 4. 6. 1–2. This occurs in relation to चित्रायाग in which seven subsidiary offerings are prescribed of which two are सारस्वत. सारस्वतौ means 'सरस्वतीदेवताकः सरस्वान्देवताकश्चेत्युभौ सारस्वतौ'. The पू. मी. सू. is 'मुख्यक्रमेणाङ्गानां तदर्थत्वात्' (V. 1. 14 वा.) याज्ञ. II. 135 mentions as heirs to a man dying sonless, his wife, daughter and parents (पितरौ). Suppose a man dies leaving his father and mother as nearest relatives, who should succeed, viz. whether mother should be preferred to father or vice versa or whether both should take the estate equally. The मिताक्षरा prefers the mother to the father, the Sm. C. refers to this instance of सारस्वतौ भवतः and there being]][[no special ground of choice between the two, holds that the father takes first following certain other smstis like Bphat-Vippu. P 297) are " Arrial : reggae Rua RT क्रमात प्रधानयोः क्रमः पञ्चमे दशितो न पुनः सारस्वतावित्येकशेषत एव कथंचित करावयक्ति रुपपादितेति निबन्धमेव (!) मातुः प्राथम्पसमर्थनम् । अत एव श्रीकरेण पित्रोधनग्रहण augthy i... alea R ehau , qui in the above passage refers to fiftb adhyāya of P. The Dāyabhāga prefers the father to the mother and several works like the Vyavahāraprakāśa, (p. 524), the Madanaratna (p. 364) do the same. The words of the Smṛtic. (II. p. 297) are 'सारस्वतौ भवतः इत्युत्पत्तिवाक्ये क्रमाश्रवणादयमाश्विनक्रमात् प्रधानयोः क्रमः पञ्चमे दर्शितो न पुनः सारस्वतावित्येकशेषत एव कथञ्चित् क्रमावगतिरुपपादितेति निबन्धेभ्य एव (!) मातुः प्राथम्यसमर्थनम् । अत एव श्रीकरेण पित्रोर्धनग्रहणे क्रमचिन्ताऽयुक्तेत्युक्तम् । ... दौहित्राभावे पितृगामित्वमवगन्तव्यम्' । 'पञ्चमे' in the above passage refers to fifth adhyāya of P. M. S.]]

[[P1316]]Details are provided as [[ragards|regards]] the two [[oiterings|offerings]] to [[Saragvati|Sarasvatī]] and [[Sarastata.|Sārasvata.]] The doubt [[arisas : ara|arises : are]] the details about the [[offer ing|offering]] to be made to the female deity to be performed first or are they to be first performed as the offerings to the male deity ? The [[prima facie. view|prima facie view]] being that as no [[sāstra|śāstra]] exists [[rogulating|regulating]] priority [[ons|one]] may do as one likes, the established conclusion is that the order of sequence in the case of the [[detaile|details]] should be determined by the order of the [[Yājyāpuvākyā verges.|Yājyānuvākyā verses.]] These are mentioned first about the [[fomnale|female]] deity in the words [['pra po devi Sarasvati'|'pra ṇo devī Sarasvatī']] (Tai. S. I. 8. 22. 1, [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VI. 61. 4). Therefore, the conclusion is that in the details also the offering to the female deity should come first.

In PMS V. 1. 16 it is decided that the order expressed in mantras should be followed in preference to the order contained in [[Brābmapa|Brāhmaṇa]] texts.[^2165] The [[Darsapūrpamāga|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] sacrifice is made up of three component sacrifices, [[Agaeya,|Āgneya,]] [[Upimsu|Upāṁśu]] and the [[Agni somlya.|Agniṣomīya.]] In Tai S. II. [[5, %. 3,|5. 2. 3,]] the [[Agoisomlya|Agniṣomīya]] sacrifice is first described and in Tai. II. 6. 3. 3 the [[Agaeya|Āgneya]] is [[described Bat|described. But]] these are held to be Brāhmaṇa texts, though now appearing in the [[Sambitā toxts,|Saṁhitā texts,]] since they lay down a vidhi; but in the [[Mantra pātha|Mantrapāṭha]] the [[Agneya|Āgneya]] mantra [['Agnir-tūrdhā' (Tai B. IIL 5.7.1)|'Agnir-mūrdhā' (Tai. B. III. 5. 7. 1)]] is first set out and then follows the mantra [['Agossomā savedasi (Tai. Br. IIL. 5, 7.2).|'Agniṣomā savedasā' (Tai. Br. III. 5. 7. 2).]] Therefore, the Āgneya is to be first [[per formed|performed]] and the [[Agaisomlya|Agniṣomīya]] afterwards.

If there are several deities or brāhmaṇas to be honoured with several acts or things, or there are several yūpas ([[ sacrificial| (sacrificial]] posts) as in [[Aikādasina|Aikādaśina]] animal sacrifice on which several [[Bamskaras|saṁskāras]] are to be performed from [[anjana|añjana]] to [[purivyāna (sur|parivyāṇa (sur-]]

[^2165]: [[Tare i n una 7: 14: \# 176**: 1 ARU arafat Home पेन हि क्रमेण वाक्यानि पठितानि सेनैव क्रमेणाधीताम्पत्य जमवन्ति । पथार्थप्रत्यय पदार्थानामनुष्ठानात् । स च पाठक्रमो द्विविधः मन्त्रपाठो माहाणपाठमा बानेपानीपोमीयो स्तनधाग्यानुवाक्याक्रमायः कम आनीयते स मन्त्रपाठाद । संचार मन्त्रपाठी बाणपाठार TEATRI HOT TUTTET ATT EMRIT *. . 4. pp. 176-177.|पदार्थानां यः क्रमः स पाठक्रमः । तस्माच्च पदार्थानां क्रम आध्रीयते । येन हि क्रमेण वाक्यानि पठितानि तेनैव क्रमेणाधीतान्यर्थप्रत्ययं जनयन्ति । यथार्थप्रत्ययं च पदार्थानामनुष्ठानात् । स च पाठक्रमो द्विविधः मन्त्रपाठो ब्राह्मणपाठश्च । तत्राग्नेयाग्नीषोमीययोस्तत्तद्याज्यानुवाक्याक्रमाद्यः क्रम आध्रीयते स मन्त्रपाठात् । स चायं मन्त्रपाठो ब्राह्मणपाठाद् बलीयान् । अनुष्ठाने ब्राह्मणवाक्यापेक्षया मन्त्रवाक्यस्यान्तरङ्गत्वात् । मी. न्या. प्र. pp. 176–177.]]

[[P1317]]rounding with a girdle), then one should perform all the [[sams. kāras|samskāras]] from añjana to parivyāṇa on the yūpa first and then all these on the 2nd and so on up to the last [[yūpā|yūpa]] or whether one should perform añjana on all yūpas one after another, the next [[sanskāra|samskāra]] on all yūpas [[ons|one]] after another and so on till the last samskāra of [[parivyāna|parivyāṇa]] is done on all yūpas one after another. The former way is called [[kāṇdānusamaya|kāṇḍānusamaya]] and the latter [['padārtbānu samaya '|'padārthānusamaya',]][^2166] Jai. V. [[2.7–9|2. 7–9]] refers to the first method and Jai. V. [[2. 1-3|2. 1–3]] to the 2nd. Vide for the explanation and illustration of these two, H. of Dh. Vol. II. pp. 739–740, p. 1132 [[n 2528|n. 2528]] and Vol. IV. pp. 441-42 [[n 987|n. 987]]. On [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 232 ([['apasavyam tataḥ kṣtvā )|'apasavyam tataḥ kṛtvā')]] the Mit. remarks that the performer of Śrāddha follows the [[kāṇdānusamaya|kāṇḍānusamaya]] method for the Vaiśvadeva brāhmaṇas i. e. gives them water for washing the feet, then [[acamana|ācamana]], seat, sandalwood paste, flowers [[&o,|&c.,]] then he should wear the sacred thread on the right shoulder and offer the upacāras to the pitrya [[brāhmanas|brāhmaṇas]].

The sixth adhyāya of PMS is a very interesting one. It deals with the various aspects of the question of [[adhikara|adhikāra]], that is, qualifications of the [[performar|performer]] of a sacrifice. It is a very extensive chapter having eight pādas like chap. III and X. A few of the numerous propositions contained therein that have influenced the works [[ou|on]] Dharmaśāstra have already been set out, such as women's right to participate in Vedic sacrifices, śūdra's ineligibility for them, the rathakāranyāya and niṣādasthapatinyāya and a [[fow|few]] more will be dealt with here. As a preliminary Jaimini establishes (in P. M. S. [[VL 1.1-3|VI. 1. 1–3]]) that in such texts as [[*one|'one]] desirous of heaven should offer the [[Darsapūrpamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] sacrifice or one should offer the [[Jyotistoma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] sacrifice' the Veda prescribes yāga for one who desires heaven i. e. heaven is the principal (or predominant) factor while yāga is subsidiary or [[subordi. Date;|subordinate;]] thereby it follows that the Vedic text lays down the [[characteristios|characteristics]] of the performer. The [[Pupṭikā|Ṭupṭīkā]] says that[^2167]

VI. 1. 3;]] [[अधिकारीति कर्मणामुपरिभावेनावस्थितः स्वामीत्यर्थः । दुष्टीका (on same); अर्थी समयों विज्ञान शामेणापर्डवस्तोधिकारी । सर्वदर्शनकौमुदी p. 103. VI.1.5. शुलो माव्याणामयः पशूनां सस्मासी भूतसंकामिणा ववव शुनश्च । तस्मादो यज्ञेऽनपतः ।ते. सं. VII. 1.1.6. सायण orxplains अनवक्रता|अधिकारीति कर्मणामुपरिभावेनावस्थितः स्वामीत्यर्थः । टुपटीका (on same); अर्थी समर्थो विद्वान् शास्त्रेणापर्युदस्तोऽधिकारी । सर्वदर्शनकौमुदी p. 103. This summarises in one place what Śabara and Kumārila state in different places e. g. न चैतदस्ति तिर्यगादीनामप्यधिकार इति । कस्य तर्हि । यः समर्थः कृत्स्नं कर्माभिनिवर्तयितुम् । न चैषां देवतान्तराभावात् । न ह्यात्मानमुद्दिश्य त्यागः सम्भवति । त्याग एवासौ न स्यात् । ... अपि च तिर्यञ्चो न कालान्तरफलेनार्थिनः । आसन्नं हि ते कामयन्ते । ... न चैते (तिर्यञ्चः) वेदमधीयते नापि स्मृतिशास्त्राणि । नाप्युपदिश्यमानमवगच्छन्ति । तस्मान्न विदुन्ति धर्मम् । अविद्वांसः कथमाधिक्रियेयुः । शबर on पू. मी. सू. VI. 1. 5. शूद्रो मनुष्याणामश्वः पशूनां तस्मात्तौ भूतसंक्रामिणौ वह्निश्च शूद्रश्च । तस्माच्छूद्रो यज्ञेऽनवक्लृप्तः । तै. सं. VII. 1. 1. 6. सायण explains अनवक्लृप्तः]] as [[यज्ञे प्रतितं न योग्य|यज्ञे प्रवर्तितुं न योग्यः]].

[[P1318]]'[[adhikari|adhikāri]] is the owner (or master) who stands above all [[aotions|actions]] ([[vāga|yāga]]).' Another more elaborate definition is given as '[[adhikari|adhikāri]] is one who desires some reward (such as heaven or happiness), who is possessed of the [[oapaoity|capacity]] to perform the act prescribed, who has learning and who is not excluded from performing a sacrifice (by [[Sruti|Śruti]])'. Even lower animals desire happiness; hence to exclude them the words 'possessed of the capacity' &c. are added. A man may be totally ignorant and so the word [['vidvan'|'vidvān']] is added; a śūdra may desire happiness, may have capacity and be also learned but he is excluded by the [[vedio Loxt,|vedic text,]] 'Therefore the [[Sudra|Śūdra]] is not fit (or ordained) for sacrifice'.

In VI. [[I. 39-40|1. 39–40]] it is established[^2168] that every man belonging to one of the three higher classes has [[&|a]] right to perform Vedic sacrifices. A man though at one time devoid of wealth may acquire wealth by various means. Similarly, it is said in VI. 1. 41 that one who is defective in a limb is just like one who [[in|is]] devoid of wealth [[i, e.|i. e.]] such a man has a right to offer [[Vedlo Bacrifices|Vedic sacrifices]] provided he takes steps to remove the weakness. In VI. 1. 42 it is further provided that if the defect is [[congential|congenital]] and incurable, the person who suffers from such a defect is not entitled to perform Vedic sacrifices.

VI. 1. 39-42;]] [[तदर्मा|तद्धर्मा]] means [['द्रव्यरहितधर्मा तत्तल्य हति'.|'द्रव्यरहितधर्मा तत्तुल्य इति'.]] The last [[sutra|sūtra]] may be paraphrased [[०|as]] [[उत्पत्ती एव येषामनवैकल्य जात्यधावीनां सर्वेषामतिसमाधेयाकल्यानां ते माधिक्रियते आग्यावेक्षणादिभिः । क्रोनित्यसंयोगात्।. . सं. VI. 3. 10. 5]] states [['जायमानो हरे पाहाणनिभिर्वाणवा जायते ब्रह्मचर्येण ऋषिग्यो यज्ञेन देवेन्यः प्रजया पितृस्य एष वा अवणी यः पुत्री यज्वा ब्रह्मचारिवासी'.|'जायमानो ह वै ब्राह्मणस्त्रिभिर्ऋणवा जायते ब्रह्मचर्येण ऋषिभ्यो यज्ञेन देवेभ्यः प्रजया पितृभ्य एष वा अनृणो यः पुत्री यज्वा ब्रह्मचारिवासी'.]] On this, [[पू.मी.ए.VI. 2. 31 माह्मणस्य सोमविधामय मणवाक्येन संयोगात्on|पू. मी. सू. VI. 2. 31 is ब्राह्मणस्य सोमविद्याप्रजननावाक्येन संयोगात् , on]] which [[शपर|शबर]] explains that the word [[brahmana|brāhmaṇa]] is only [[Mastrative 'घाडणग्रहणं प्रदर्शनार्थम् । आयमानी मागणी राजम्पो वैश्यबेलिया नापमानो जातश्चेति ।।|illustrative 'ब्राह्मणग्रहणं तु प्रदर्शनार्थम् । जायमानो ब्राह्मणो राजन्यो वैश्यश्चेति । तथा जायमानो जातश्चेति ।'.]]

[[P1319]]The ancient and medieval Hindu law of inheritance and [[BUOopssion|succession]] was modelled on this. Vide [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] II. 140, Manu IX. 201 and [[Narada|Nārada]] (dāyabhāga, verses 21-22). [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] II. 140 [[declarea|declares]] that the impotent, the outcast and his son, a lame man, a [[lunatio,|lunatic,]] an idiot, a blind man and a person afflicted with an [[inourable diseage|incurable disease]] are not entitled to a share, but are entitled to receive maintenance. This subject has been dealt with in [[of Dh.|H. of Dh.]] Vol. [[II.|III.]] pp. 610-612. The [[Mit,|Mit.]] on Yāj. II. 140 provides that the grounds of disqualification apply to males and females alike. But the recent Hindu Succession Act (No. 32 of 1956) [[sweepg|sweeps]] away all these grounds of disqualification by providing that no person shall be disqualified from succeeding to any property on the ground of any disease, defect or deformity or, save as provided in this [[act,|Act,]] on any other ground whatever (section 28).

A good many sūtras (in chap. VI. 3. [[17-41)|17–41)]] deal with what are called 'pratinidhi' (substitute, representative or proxy). These have been described in H. of Dh. Vol. II. pp. [[684"|684,]] 1110, 1203, Vol. III, pp. 471, 637, [[653. 654|653, 654]] (where Satyāṣāḍha Śr. S. III containing similar rules is mentioned). A few of them are briefly mentioned here. The first rule is that if the substance declared by the Veda for preparing an offering [[parishes|perishes]] or is lost as regards an obligatory rite or as regards [[kāmya|a kāmya]] rite that is already begun, one should substitute another substance ([[liks|like]] nivāras, wild rice grains) for [[vrihi|vrīhi]] (rice grains) or yava [[i.e,|i. e.]] barley grains and finish the rite (VI. 3. 13–17). In some cases Vedic texts [[presoribe|prescribe]] a substitute for the substance required to be used, as in '[['if|‘if]] he (the performer) cannot obtain the Soma plant, he should substitute [[pūtika|pūtīka]] stalks and [[extraot|extract]] juice [[therefrom.'|therefrom.’]] It is argued by the [[objeotor|objector]] that, as the Veda expressly substitutes [[pātikas|pūtīkas]] for soma, one should conclude that where [[Voda|Veda]] does not expressly mention [[&|a]] substitute for [[&|a]] prescribed substance, it means that a substitute is not allowed by the Veda in other cases. The siddhānta is that the prescribing of [[pūtikas|pūtīkas]] as substitute is a restrictive rule[^2169] [[vie|viz.]] that although [[sovoral|several]] plants may resemble soma, there is [[&|a]] restriction that only [[pūtikas|pūtīkas]] should be substituted. It is provided (in [[III. 6. 37, 39)|VI. 3. 37, 39)]] that when a substitute like [[nsvāras|nivāras]] is employed, the subsidiary [[aots|acts]] of sprinkling with water, pounding with mortar and pestle that are performed on rice or barley grains should also be per-

[^2169]: [[Fargare merger: I VI. 3. 16; HITATA Fuig shg foran: finca rrireit garfirulacant ma i ur. The UGTETOT (IX. 5. 3) provides|नियमार्थः क्वचिद्विधिः । VI. 3. 16; सोमाभावे बहुषु सदृशेषु प्राप्तेषु नियमः क्रियते पूतीका एवाभिनेतव्या इति । शबर. The ताण्ड्यब्राह्मण (IX. 5. 3) provides]] '[['oft hefugi'|‘यदि सोमं न विन्देयुः’]] which is quoted in n. [[2001|2101]] [[abovo.|above.]]

[[P1320]]formed on them. When rice grains are [[amployed|employed]] the mantra expressly speaks of the rice offering as the sap or essence of [[rios graing.|rice grains.]] [[There|Their]] adaptation (ūha) is to be made [[an 'pivāranim|as 'nivārāṇām]] medha' (vide PMS IX. 3. 1-2).[^2170] But in certain cases no substitute is allowed viz. the devatā with respect to which an havis (offering) is prescribed cannot have another substituted [[iz &|in a]] rite e. g. 'Āgneyoṣṭakapālaḥ' cannot be changed into [['aindrostakapālaħ',|'aindroṣṭakapālaḥ',]] since in that case the rite will cease to be what was intended. Similarly, when the text says [['be|'he]] offers into the Āhavanīya fire,' one cannot substitute the [[Gārbapatya fra:|Gārhapatya fire;]] another mantra cannot be substituted for the one prescribed [[por|nor]] can other [[sites|rites]] be substituted for the Prayājas [['gamidho yajati'|'samidho yajati']] &c.).[^2171]

The Veda forbids the use of varakas, [[kodrains|kodravas]] and māṣas as unfit for a sacrifice. If a person mistakenly takes [[maṣa|māṣa]] grains or particles believing them to be [[mudya graing|mudga grains]] or particles and uses them in a sacrifice in which an oblation of boiled mudga pulse is prescribed, he will not be performing the desired [[rita|rite]] as what is forbidden as unfit cannot be [[&|a]] substitute.[^2172]

This [[pyāya|nyāya]] is relied upon by the [[Mit|Mit.]] on Yāj. II. 126 (which simply states that if joint family properties are suppressed or concealed by some members and kept for themselves, they should be distributed in equal shares even after partition (when this is found out), which holds that this verse cannot be interpreted so as to absolve the concealer of such joint property from guilt simply on the ground that he was himself a part owner and explains that just as a sacrificer mistakenly believing māṣa particles as mudga ones loses the fruit of the sacrifice, [[80|so]] the concealer of joint property is guilty of wrong. The Vyavahāraprakāśa (p. 555) and Aparārka p. 732 take the same view, but the [[Dāyabbāga|Dāyabhāga]] (XII. 11-13) and V. R. (p. 526) are opposed to this (vide H. of Dh. Vol. III. p. 636). The [[Prayasoittatattva|Prāyaścittatattva]] p. 482 has an elaborate note on this nyāya.

[^2170]: [[On a Hont aims at a ... naras tierunt CHATYATA Fai Ta on IX. 3. 1. This is a. m. VII, 7, 5. 2-3. free means FITTIG.|अस्ति तु प्रकृतौ व्रीहिलिङ्गो मन्त्रः—स्योनं ते सदनं ... प्रतितिष्ठ व्रीहीणां मेध सुमनस्यमान इति । शबर on IX. 3. 1. This is तै. ब्रा. VII. 7. 5. 2-3. मेध means सारभूत.]]

The n may be explained as maiore Aran a neat THIE mitateur werPAT fortugra: 1. The . #. V. 1. 8. 1 has t 1973°, vide H. of Dh. vol. III. P. 637 and note 1209 for Jaimiai's sūtra and the quotation from the Mit.|प्रतिषिद्धं चाविशेषेण हि तच्छ्रुतिः । VI. 3. 20; प्रतिषिद्धं च न प्रतिनिधिधातव्यमिति । अविशेषेण ह्येतदुच्यते—न यज्ञा माषा वरका कोद्रवाश्चेति । शबर. The सूत्र may be explained as प्रतिषिद्धं माषादिकं न प्रतिनिधेयं यस्मात् अविशेषेण यज्ञसम्बन्धमात्रे निषेधश्रुतिः ।. The तै. सं. V. 1. 8. 1 has 'अमेध्या वै माषाः', vide H. of Dh. vol. III. p. 637 and note 1209 for Jaimini's sūtra and the quotation from the Mit.]]

[[P1321]]Another rule is that [[there oan|there can]] be no substitute for the performer of the sacrifice (VI. 3. 21), since (in Jai. [[TII,|III.]] 7. 18–20) it is laid down that the fruit of the rite belongs to the owner, though after beginning the rite he leaves everything to the priests engaged to perform the [[rito.|rite.]] The only exception is in the case of sattras (Jai. VI. [[3.22 )|3. 22)]] performed by many persons together acting both as performers and as priests.

One important adhikarana is VI. 7. 31-40. There is a Sattra called 'Viśvasṛjām-ayanam' which is said to last for 1000 samvatsaras. After [[referrring|referring]] to Tai. Br. I. [[3.7.7|3. 7. 7]] and I. 7. 6. 2 (śatāyuḥ puruṣaḥ) and to the views of [[Kārsnājini|Kārṣṇājini]] and [[Lāvuka. yani,|Lāvukāyani,]] Jaimini boldly establishes the conclusion that [[sanvat sara|samvatsara]] here means day. Vide H. of Dh. [[VOL.|Vol.]] II p. 1246 n. 2683 for the Mahābhāṣya stating that the Yājñikas in speaking about such sattras only follow the tradition handed down by ancient [[Bages.|sages.]] [[Modhatithi|Medhātithi]] on Manu I. 84 ([['vedoktam-ayur-martyānām )|'vedoktam-āyur-martyānām')]] has a long note wherein he refers to the [[viw|view]] of Jaimini, [[quotos|quotes]] [['satāyur|'śatāyur]] vai puruṣaḥ' and also [['satam-innu|'śatam-innu]] sarado anti devā' (Rg. I. 89. 9) and [[oites|cites]] the views of other interpreters. The Kātyāyana Śr. (I. 6. 17–27) discusses this very subject at great length, refers to the differing interpretations of Bharadvāja, Kārṣṇājini and Laugākṣi but ultimately holds that samvatsara means 'day' here.

In the first six chapters of the P. M. S. the procedure of such rites as [[Darsapūrṇaināsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] the details of which are expressly laid down by the Veda has been considered. In the six chapters from the 7th consideration will be given to sacrifices like Aindrāgna, the procedure of which is not expressly laid down by the Veda.[^2173] In the 7th chapter what is considered is whether in the [[vikśtis|vikṛtis]] (sacrifices that are modifications of the model [[Bacrifices)|sacrifices)]] details have to be added and, if so, which and how many of the details have to be carried out.

The 7th chapter deals with the general question of the extension or transference of details in general (i. e. atideśa in general) to Aindrāgna and other sacrifices. Atideśa is the

[^2173]: [[The FTCMTHITT (In G. O. S.) 4th qira p. 257 says: qe arrat पदेशिकाङ्गसम्बन्धमकारः सप्रमाणकः पूर्वाध्यायषद्कगोचर प्रदर्शितः । इदानीमातिदेशिकार सम्बन्धविधान प्रतिपाद्यते । द्विविधं हि शब्दजातमुपदेशात्मकमतिदेशात्मकं चाहत्थमिदं कर्तग्य मिट्युपदेशः, तदिदं कर्वष्यमित्यतिदेशः।': अतिदेशो नाम ये परत्र विहिता धर्मास्तमतीस्थाग्य तेषां वेशः ।... यथा देववववधज्ञवसो भोजयितव्य इति । श्लोकमप्युवाहरन्ति । प्रकृतात्कर्मणो परमाससमानेषु कर्म। धर्मप्रदेशो येन स्यात् सोऽतिदेश इति स्थिति इति । स च.नामा rreth 1 garera 997 ATE OF ITHT ON . VII, 1. 12.|The न्यायत्नमाला (in G. O. S.) 4th परिच्छेद p. 257 says: 'एवं तावदौपदेशिकाङ्गसम्बन्धप्रकारः सप्रमाणकः पूर्वाध्यायषट्कगोचरः प्रदर्शितः । इदानीमातिदेशिकाङ्गसम्बन्धविधानं प्रतिपाद्यते । द्विविधं हि शब्दजातमुपदेशात्मकमतिदेशात्मकं च। ‘इत्थमिदं कर्तव्य’मित्युपदेशः, ‘तद्वदिदं कर्तव्य’मित्यतिदेशः ।' ; अतिदेशो नाम ये परत्र विहिता धर्मास्तमपरित्यज्य तेषां देशः । ... यथा देवदत्तवद्यज्ञदत्तो भोजयितव्य इति । श्लोकमप्युदाहरन्ति । प्रकृतात्कर्मणो यस्मात्समानेषु च कर्मसु । धर्मप्रदेशो येन स्यात् सोऽतिदेश इति स्थितिः ॥ इति । स च नाम्ना वचनेन वा । वचनं पुनर्द्विविधं प्रत्यक्षश्रुतमानुमानिकं च । शबर on जै. VII. 1. 12.]]

[[P1322]]process or method by which the details prescribed in connection with one rite are taken beyond that rite and transferred to another. Śabara quotes from some ancient author a śloka defining atideśa. The sacrifice from which details are transferred is called [[praksli|prakṛti]] (model or Archetype) and the sacrifice to [[whlob|which]] details are transferred or extended is [[oalled|called]] [[vikrti|vikṛti]] (or [[Eotype ).|Ectype).]] Atideśa may be provided for by vacana (Vedic text) or by the name. The first is of two kinds viz. by a direct statement or by an inferential process. For example, as regards a [[magio saorifice|magic sacrifice]] called Iṣu the text says, after speaking of some details, that the rest are the same as in[^2174] Śyena. An instance of an inferential [[racina|vacana]] is the extension to the Saurya sacrifice of the details of the Āgneya in [[Darsapūrṇamāsa,|Darśapūrṇamāsa,]] because both are very closely connected and because no details are prescribed by the text about 'Sauryayāga' (PMS VII. 4. 1). The name also is two-fold, viz. name of a rite and name of a [[rainskāra.|samskāra.]] The Māsāgnihotra prescribed (vide PMS II. 3. 24) in [[Kundapāyi nām-ayana|Kuṇḍapāyinām-ayana]] is a different rite from the obligatory Agnihotra (as in 'yāvajjīvam-agnihotram [[jubuyāt')|juhuyāt')]] as proved above, but the [[Dane'agnihotra'|name 'agnihotra']] being common to both, the details of the usual Agnihotra (such as milking the cow, offering curds or milk, using a Khadira fuel stick &c.) are to be extended to [[Māsāgai. hotra|Māsāgnihotra]] (Jai. [[VI. 3.1-4).|VII. 3. 1–4).]] Extension due to samskāra name is instanced in Jai. VII. 3. 12–15. In the [[Varupapraghāna|Varuṇapraghāsa]] (one of the [[cāturmāgyas),|cāturmāsyas),]] [[Avabhstha|Avabhṛtha]] (bath) [[ia|is]] prescribed, but no details are added and therefore the requisite details are to [[ba|be]] taken from the rules about the [[Avabhștha|Avabhṛtha]] in Somayāga.[^2175]

Atideśa is frequently resorted to by smṛtis and [[digesta.|digests.]] For example, Yāj. I. 236 and 242 extend the procedure of [[Pindam pitryajña|Piṇḍapitṛyajña]] as regards [[Agnaukarang|Agnaukaraṇa]] and offering of [[pindas|piṇḍas]] to the [[Parvanaśrāddha.|Pārvaṇaśrāddha.]] The Parāśarasmṛti (VII. 18–19) speaks[^2176]

II. 1. pp. 168-169) remarks 'चण्डाल्यादिगमने यावान् प्रत्यवायस्तावानुदक्यागमने इत्यभिप्रेत्य तैर्नामभिर्व्यवहारः । यथा कुण्डपायिनामयने मासमाग्निहोत्रं जुहोति इत्यविशेषनामनिर्देशेन नित्याग्निहोत्रधर्मा विधीयन्ताद्वत्']]

[[P1323]]of a woman in her monthly illness as a [[cīṇdall|cāṇḍālī]] on the first day of the illness, as the [[murderegs|murderess]] of a [[brahmana|brāhmaṇa]] on the 2nd day and as a washerwoman on the 3rd day. The Par. M. remarks that the intention of calling such a woman by these names is to convey that intercourse with her will result in the same sin as would be incurred by a person of higher classes if he had intercourse with a [[cāndals &.|cāṇḍālī &c.]] For an instance of express extension, vide V. M. pp. 56–57 when it quotes a verse of [[Pitamaha|Pitāmaha]] on the balance ordeal (in which the word [['yūpayat'|'yūpavat']] is used) and remarks that [[atidesa|atideśa]] is prescribed expressly by the word [['yūpą vat.|'yūpavat'.]]

The 8th chapter deals with specific cases of extension (atideśa). The [[Darsapūrṇamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] is the prakṛti of all[^2177] [[istis|iṣṭis]] and [['Darsapūrpamāsābhyām yajeta'|'Darśapūrṇamāsābhyām yajeta']] is called vidhyādi and vidhyanta is the whole procedure of [[Darsapūrnamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] (except the originative injunction [['darśapūrnamāsyābhyām yajeta)|'darśapūrṇamāsyābhyām yajeta']]) detailed in the Brāhmaṇas about the offering of [[purodāsa|puroḍāśa]] (cake) &c. In the [[Vikstiyāga|Vikṛtiyāga]] called Saurya[^2178] the [[sontence|sentence]] 'one who desires to secure the lustre of Vedic learning should offer boiled rice to the Sun' is the vidhyādi, but no details are mentioned there. There is an expectation of some procedure and, though there are numerous vidhyantas in relation to sacrifices, the special word [['#nirvapati'|'nirvapati']] is indicative of the procedure of [[Darsapūrnamasa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] (in which also there is nirvāpa) and one comes to understand that the Saurya caru is offered as in Āgneya (the first rite in

[^2177]: Vedic sacrifices are usually divided for convenience into [[thred|three]] varieties, viz. [[Isti|Iṣṭi]] (in which offerings are of [[mūk,|milk,]] clarified butter, rice, barley and other [[graiss ), Pasa|grains), Paśu]] and Soma and the latter again into [[Ekaba|Ekāha]] (lasting for one day like [[Agaistoma ), Abina|Agniṣṭoma), Ahīna]] (lasting for more than one day up to 12 days) and Sattra (lasting from 12 days to a year or more). [[Sabara|Śabara]] on PMS IV. 4. 20 states that there are four [[Mabāyajñas, vit. Agaihatra, Dardapāraamāsa, Jyotistoma, Piṇdapitryajāa.|Mahāyajñas, viz. Agnihotra, Darśapūrṇamāsa, Jyotiṣṭoma, Piṇḍapitṛyajña.]] There are seven Soma sacrifices as stated by Gaut. Dh. S. VIII. 18. Besides [[thess|these]] Śrauta rites, there are other rites prescribed in the gṛhyasūtras, which are offered in gṛhya fire and seven important ones [[from which are called pakayajñas,|from which are called pākayajñas.]] Vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. pp. 193–194.

VIL 4. 103 बेदेपि दर्शपूर्णमासाम्यो बजेति विष्यादिः। विष्यन्तोऽपि प्रधानविधिवजिवे करने पौराडाशिकं बाह्यणम्, तेन समेतोऽयं विष्यादिविशिधापूर्वनिचि प्रति पुरुष प्रवर्सयति । तस्मा tserra: i fremt; on g. A. & VIII. 1. 2 Te explains: Forsvaret odt विष्यादि सोपि पदिकेन विष्यन्तेनायुमानाद पुखी संनिहितेनेकवाक्यतां चास्यति । पथा सोफे चई निषेपेदनहावर्चसकाम इति। चत्र कथमिति विध्यतापेकापामनेकविष्यत्तसंनिपातेडसा धारणेन निर्षपतिशब्देन वापौर्णमासिकविण्यन्तसंबोगिना लिोन नदीयो विष्याता सम्यते .|विध्यन्तो वा प्रकृतिवच्चोदनाया प्रवर्तेत तथाहि लिङ्गदर्शनम् । पू. मी. सू. VIII. 4. 10; वेदेऽपि दर्शपूर्णमासाभ्यां यजेतेति विध्यादिः । विध्यन्तोऽपि प्रधानविधिवर्जिते कृत्स्ने पौरोडाशिकं ब्राह्मणम्, तेन समेतोऽयं विध्यादिविशिष्टापूर्वविधिं प्रति पुरुषं प्रवर्तयति । तस्मात्सोऽस्यान्तः । शबर; on पू. मी. सू. VIII. 1. 2 शबर explains: विध्यन्तापेक्षो यो वैकृतो विध्यादिः सोऽपि वैदिकेन विध्यन्तेनानुमानाद् बुद्धौ संनिहितेनैकवाक्यतां यास्यति । यथा सौर्यं चरुं निर्वपेद्ब्रह्मवर्चसकाम इति । तत्र कथमिति विध्यन्तापेक्षायामनेकविध्यन्तसंनिपातेऽसाधारणेन निर्वपतिशब्देन दर्शपूर्णमासिकविध्यन्तसंयोगिना लिङ्गेन तदीयो विध्यन्तः प्रसज्यते ।]]

[[P1324]]Darśapūrṇamāsa). The details of the prakṛti are to be performed in all other iṣṭis along with the few details that may have been stated expressly as to some.

The Jyotiṣṭoma is the prakṛti of all soma sacrifices of one day and of Dvādaśāha and its details are performed in all modifications of soma sacrifices such as Atirātra. The Agniṣomīya is the prakṛti of all sacrifices in which a paśu (animal) is to be sacrificed, the details of which are to be performed in all vikṛtis of paśuyāgas. Dvādaśāha is of two kinds, Ahīna and Sattra and is the prakṛti of all Ahīna sacrifices like Dvirātra, Trirātra up to Śatarātra; and Dvādaśāha of the sattra type is the model of all sattras. Gavām-ayana is the prakṛti of all sacrifices like Ādityānām-ayana. Darvihomas are sui juris and are not prakṛtis or vikṛtis of any other sacrifices. All these are considered in the 8th chapter.

The 9th chapter deals with ūha (adaptation). When applying the principle of atideśa certain alterations and adaptations are necessary in the matter of mantras, sāmans and samskāras. The word ūha ordinarily means only tarka or vicāraṇā (reasoning), yet in PMS it has a special meaning.[^2179]

The Āgneya is the prakṛti in which the nirvāpa (offering) is to be made with the words 'I offer to Agni what is liked by him'; in the Sauryayāga, which is a vikṛti of Āgneya, the offering has to be made with the words 'I offer to Sūrya what is liked by him.' In the Vājapeya we read 'he offers to Bṛhaspati nirvāpa grains cooked on seventeen pans'. The Vājapeya is a modification of Darśapūrṇamāsa in which the grains of rice are sprinkled with water; therefore sprinkling has to be done on nivāra grains also (PMS IX. 2. 40). On the 2nd and following days of the Jyotiṣṭoma sacrifice there is recitation of the Subrahmaṇyā litany[^2180] addressed to Indra by the Subrahmaṇya priest, beginning with the words 'Indra āgaccha, [[bariya|hariva]] āgaccha, [[Medhatither-mesa|Medhātither-meṣa]] &c'. In the Agniṣṭut sacrifice also there is

IX. 1. 44.]]

[[P1325]][[Subrahmanya-nigada 1181|Subrahmaṇyā-nigada]][^2181] addressed to Agni. In reciting it the word 'Agne' is substituted for 'Indra'; but the following words 'Hariva [[āgaccha'is|āgaccha']] are not to be changed and are to be [[recited. As|recited, as]] they are attributes which may be predicated of Agni also (PMS [[IX.I. 42-44).|IX. 1. 42–44).]] The principle deduced by the [[Mimamsakas|Mīmāmsakas]] seems to be that, only if the words in the original mantra cannot at all be extended to the modified yāga as they stand, [[thon|then]] ūha may have to be resorted to but not otherwise. [[Sabara|Śabara]] however, notes that the [['yājõikas|'yājñikas]] perform ūha (i. e. adapt them by suitable [[changes) i.e.they|changes) i. e. they]] recite 'agne āgaccha [[rohitasva|rohitāśva]] [[brhad-bhano'|bṛhad-bhāno']] etc.). It is noteworthy that, according to PMS II. 1. 34 and [[Sabara|Śabara]] thereon, a mantra as adapted ([[whita|ūhita]]) is not called a mantra, since only those are mantras[^2182] that are recognized as such by the learned. In the [[Darsapūrpamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] when the priest takes out four handfuls of grains and puts them in a winnowing basket he repeats over three of the handfuls the mantra which literally means 'at the command of God Savitṛ, with the arms of [[Asvins|Aśvins]] and with the hands of [[Pūsan|Pūṣan]] I take out for Agni thee that art dear (to him)'.[^2183] The PMS holds (in IX. 1. [[36-37 )|36–37)]] that the words Savitṛ, Pūṣan, [[Asvin|Aśvin]] are not to be changed by [[ūba|ūha]] in the modifications of [[Darsapūrṇamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] where the deity to which the offering is to be made is not Agni. [[Sabara agsigns|Śabara assigns]] rather far-fetched meanings to the words Savitṛ, [[Aspins|Aśvins]] and Pūṣan (as in the note below) and says that they are meant to eulogize the laying aside of the handfuls of grains for offering. There is another interesting case where there is no

II. 6. 4. 1. देवस्य सवितुः प्रसवे देवेन सवित्रा यजमानेन प्रस्ते, उदित वा सवितरि। अश्विनो हुम्या परिक्रयमभिप्रेत्य यजमानयोर्वन्यस्यो हुभ्याम् । अश्विनौ हि तो अनचाधेयेऽश्व वानेन, अशितबन्तोषा तयज्ञफलमनवाते। तबाह इति स्वौ पाहू न्यपविशति । देवताध्वर्योऽधि नोरेतोषाह इति । पूषणं तु यजमानमेव मन्यते पुष्णात । एवं सर्व निर्धापप्रकाशनार्याः । शबर on Ix. 1.37.|स्तौ दर्शपूर्णमासौ । तत्र निर्वापमन्त्रः । देवस्य त्वा सवितुः प्रसवेऽश्विनोर्बाहुभ्यां पूष्णो हस्ताभ्यामग्नये जुष्टं निर्वपामीति । शबर on IX. 1. 36; देवस्य ... निर्वपामि occurs in तै. सं. II. 6. 4. 1. देवस्य सवितुः प्रसवे—देवेन सवित्रा यजमानेन प्रसूते, उदिते वा सवितरि । अश्विनोर्बाहुभ्यां परिक्रयमभिप्रेत्य यजमानयोर्वाऽध्वर्योर्बाहुभ्याम् । अश्विनौ हि तौ अग्न्याधेयेऽश्वदानेन, अशितवन्तौ वा तयज्ञफलमश्नवाते । तद्बाहू इति स्वौ बाहू व्यपदिशति । देवताध्वर्यूऽश्विनौरेतौ बाहू इति । पूषणं तु यजमानमेव मन्यते पुष्णाति । एवं सर्वे निर्वापप्रकाशनार्थाः । शबर on IX. 1. 37.]] The words [['परिक्रयमभिमेत्य'|'परिक्रयमभिप्रेत्य']] refer to the fact that the priest adhvaryu is engaged by the [[yajamāda|yajamāna]] as [[agant|agent]] for [[a payment|payment]] of fee and therefore [[adhvaryu's|adhvaryu’s]] arms [[aro|are]] [[yajamāna's|yajamāna’s]] arms.



[[P1326]]ūha. In the [[Dargapūrpamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] there is a [[praiga (direotion)|praiṣa (direction)]] 'set [[dowa|down]][^2184] the water for sprinkling, put down the fuel [[stioks|sticks]] and the bunch of kuśa grass, [[olean|clean]] the [[sruc and fruva|sruc and śruva]] ladles, gird up the wife (of the sacrificer) and come out with clarified butter'. Suppose the sacrificer has two or more [[wivos.|wives.]] Still the singular [['patnla'|'patnīm']] was to be used and not the dual or plural either in the model itself (IX. 3. 20) or in any modification thereof (IX. 3. 21). Dharmaśāstra works make use of ūha. The [[Viṣṇu dharmasūtra|Viṣṇudharmasūtra]] provides that one should perform [[Sraddha|Śrāddha]] of one's maternal grand-father and the latter's two male ancestors in a similar way by the ūha of the mantra.[^2185] The mantra in the case of the śrāddha of male ancestors is [['Sundhantam pitarah|'Śundhantām pitaraḥ']] ([[Ap. Śr.|Āp. Śr.]] I. 7. 13), which should be changed into [['Sundbantim mātāmabāḥ';|'Śundhantām mātāmahāḥ';]] vide Mit. on [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 254 and H. of Dh. Vol. IV p. 513 [[n. 11428.|n. 1142a.]]

When in a sacrifice boiled rice is to be offered the mantra is [['ayonam... Vrihlpām medha sumanasyamānah|'syonam...vrīhīṇām medha sumanasyamānaḥ']] (vide [[pp. 1898-99 2. 2125|pp. 1298–99 n. 2125]] above). If boiled rice is destroyed or not available and [[nivūra|nivāra]] grains are substituted, there is no ūha as [['n vārdpām medha'|'nivārāṇām medha']] but the words [['vribiṇām medha'|'vrīhīṇām medha']] are to be retained (PMS IX. 3. 23–26), because, as stated in PMS [[VI 3. 27|VI. 3. 27]] ([[sāmānyam tac-cik[rṣā bi ), olvāras|sāmānyam tac-cikīrṣā hi), nivāras]] are substituted on [[acoount|account]] of the similarity of parts of boiled nivāras with the parts of rice grains.

In the third and fourth [[padas|pādas]] of the 9th chapter there are twelve [[adhikaranas|adhikaraṇas]] about the [[adhrigu-praisa|adhrigu-praiṣa]] recited by the [[Hots|Hotṛ]] in [[Pasubandha|Paśubandha.]] Vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. p. 1121, note 2504 for that [[praiga.|praiṣa.]] There is ūha to be made about certain [[wordø|words]] therein and PMS explains some of the unfamiliar and difficult words in that text.

The [[tenth, chapter|tenth chapter]] is the longest chapter in the PMS, having eight pādas and 577 sūtras (i. e. more than one-fifth of

The next sūtra is विकृतौ चापि तद्वचनात् । IX. 3. 21.]]

[[P1327]]the total number of [[sūtras )|sūtras)]] just as the third (with 363 [[sutras )|sūtras)]] and the 6th (with 349 [[sutras )|sūtras)]] have eight pādas each. This chapter is concerned with bādha (annulment or exclusion) and abhyuccaya or samuccaya which is the exact opposite or anti-thesis of bādha. The general rule is that the details of the prakṛtiyāga (model sacrifice) should be taken over in the vikṛti (modification). But in some cases the vikṛti-yāga[^2186] has a different name, some of the samskāras (purificatory and embellishing acts) and some of the substances (dravya) employed in the prakṛti cannot be extended to the vikṛtis, because there are express texts negativing their employment or because they serve no purpose or have become useless or senseless. Śabara states that bādha occurs when an idea or knowledge determined for certain as this type or that has to be understood as wrong owing to some (special) reason while abhyuccaya (addition or combination) occurs when even after we have the knowledge that certain matters are to be extended to the vikṛti there is the further idea that a few others also are to be additional in the vikṛti.

The Mai. S. prescribes that one desirous[^2187] of long life should offer a lump of one hundred kṛṣṇalas (golden pieces shaped as grains of rice) heated in clarified butter. But no pounding (avaghāta, threshing) is to be done in this, as the grains being made of gold, there is no chaff which can be separated by pounding (X. 1. 1–3). Similarly, no [[upastarana|upastaraṇa]][^2188] (pouring of clarified butter) and abhighāraṇa (the subsequent pouring of clarified butter) are to be done, since in the model sacrifice these two are done for making the oblation to have a sweet flavour (X. 2. 3–11). A caru of rice has to be boiled (i. e. the heat of fire is to be applied to it). In the same way the golden pieces are to be heated in clarified butter by fire (X. 2. [[1-2 ).|1–2).]] The golden pieces have to be sucked like sugarcane pieces (X. 2. 13–16), because they cannot be eaten while in the

[^2188]: For [[उपस्तरण and अभिधारण, vide|उपस्तरण and अभिघारण, vide]] H. of Dh. vol. II. p. 528 [[a 1233.|n. 1233.]]

[[P1328]]model sacrifice iḍā and [[prāśitra 2189|prāśitra]][^2189] are actually eaten. In a magic rite like [[śyena|Śyena]] reeds are spread on the ground and not kuśa grass (as in the model sacrifice). This bādha is due to a special [[text. 2190|text.]][^2190] The general rule in vedic sacrifices is that priests have to be chosen [[aud dakṣiṇā|and dakṣiṇā]] is to be paid to them at the end but sattras are an exception, since in sattras all are priests as well as performers (yajamānas). Here the exclusion of varaṇa (choosing) is due to the fact that in the other sacrifices the yajamāna and priests are different and the latter are engaged for fee. There is a visible purpose in selecting or engaging [[priests, 2191|priests,]][^2191] but in a sattra where ex hypothesi all are yajamānas and priests as well, there is no visible purpose in going through the ceremony of choosing priests (ṛtvig-varaṇa).

One example of samuccaya may be given. In the Vājapeya (which is said to be a form of Jyotiṣṭoma according to PMS III. 7. 50–51) seventeen animals are to be sacrificed. In the model sacrifice (i. e. Jyotiṣṭoma) also there are certain animals to be offered. The question is whether there is bādha of the paśus prescribed in the prakṛtiyāga or whether there is combination (samuccaya). The conclusion is that there is samuccaya (X. 4. 6), because in the Tai. Br. there occur passages like this—"The Brahmavādins say 'why is it that all sacrificial rites are contained in Vājapeya'? He should reply 'by means of paśus, viz. he sacrifices an animal to Agni, thereby he contains Agniṣṭoma, he contains the Ukthya &c.'" This shows that he has to sacrifice also other animals besides the [[seventeen. 2192|seventeen.]][^2192] The Mit.

[[P1329]]on Yāj. III. 243 furnishes examples of samuccaya in the matter of expiations.

The Tantravārtika (on PMS III. 3. 14 pp. 859–860) [[collecks|collects]] together about three dozen cases of bādha in general not dependent on either the conflict between śruti, [[linga &o.|liṅga &c.]] or not falling under the bādha instanced above. They are 'inference and such appearances as mirage are dispelled by or invalidated by direct perception, deceptive means of knowledge by the six means of knowledge, smṛti by śruti, a [[contradiotory|contradictory]] and [[untrust worthy|untrustworthy]] smṛti by a trustworthy and uncontradicted smṛti, a smṛti with a visible purpose by another with an unseen purpose [[&o.'.|&c.'.]]

There is in the 10th chapter an important topic about dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fees). In X. 2. 22–28 it is established that dakṣiṇā is to be given not for an unseen purpose (adṛṣṭa) but for engaging the priests to perform rites required in sacrifices. In [[II. 8 1-2|III. 8. 1–2]] it is established that the yajamāna (the svāmin) is to engage priests for sacrifices except where [[&|a]] vedic text expressly directs otherwise (as in Tai. [[8.|S.]] V. [[2.8.2).|2. 8. 2).]] In X. 3. 39 the items of [[dakśiṇā|dakṣiṇā]] are set out from [[Tāṇdya|Tāṇḍya]] XVI. 1. 10–11; it is held that the words [['dyādaśasatam|'dvādaśaśatam]] dakṣiṇā' [[mean 2193|mean]][^2193] that the cows are to be 112 (X. 3. 39, 49) and in X. 3. 50 it is provided that the [[yajamān,|yajamāna]] should himself distribute the fees and X. 3. 55 sets out the method of distribution. All priests are not to receive equal shares. The four principal priests are [[hot,|hotṛ,]] adhvaryu,[^2194] [[udgāt;|udgātṛ]] and brahmā and each of these four [[hava|have]] three assistants arranged in a certain order set out in the note below. Suppose there are one hundred cows to be distributed

There is great divergence of views about the daksinā to be givon ; vide H, of Dh, vol. There is great divergence of views about the dakṣiṇā to be given; vide H. of Dh. vol. II. p. 1188.]]

[^2194]: [[EUAT Haroulisstareita , ter AraGERTE aereat, Tu groeit tina: dret, BEAT enten siete al meno: Pet 1. The four principal priests are poderlined, three assistants come after each principal one. The assistants immediately after all four priacipals are called ardhinah (they got half of their principal, they being Harraut, wa TRATAT, GMT and ear), the thirds in each group of four constitute what are calicd trtiyinak (they get 3 of what their principals get, they being 1974, aer, smeti, magat), the last in each of the four groups are called pādinaḥ (they get one-tourth of what their principale get, they being मावस्तुत, उलेता, पोता and va).|होता मैत्रावरुणोऽच्छावाको ग्रावस्तोतृ, अध्वर्युः प्रतिप्रस्थाता नेष्टोन्नेता, ब्रह्मा ब्राह्मणाच्छंसी आग्नीध्रः पोता, उद्गाता प्रस्तोता प्रतिहर्ता सुब्रह्मण्यः-इति । The four principal priests are underlined, three assistants come after each principal one. The assistants immediately after all four principals are called ardginaḥ (they get half of their principal, they being मैत्रावरुण, प्रतिप्रस्थाता, ब्राह्मणाच्छंसी and प्रस्तोता), the thirds in each group of four constitute what are called tṛtīyinaḥ (they get ⅓ of what their principals get, they being अच्छावाकः, नेष्टा, आग्नीध्रः, प्रतिहर्ता), the last in each of the four groups are called pādinaḥ (they get one-fourth of what their principals get, they being ग्रावस्तोतृ, उन्नेता, पोता and सुब्रह्मण्य).]] Vide H. of Dh. vol. II. pp. 1188–1189 and vol. III, p. 469 for distribution of [[foes|fees]] among sacrificial [[priesta.|priests.]]

[[P1330]]as fee. Each group of four is assigned 1/4 i. e. 25 for [[hota|hotā]] and his assistants, the hotā getting [[1%|12]] and the other three getting [[reapectively|respectively]] 6, 4, and 3 i. e. they get respectively half, 1/3 and 1/4 of what their principal gets. The [[same'|same]] method applies to the other three groups. The [[prima facio|prima facie]][^2195] [[viow|view]] is that there should be an equal [[distribition|distribution]] of the fee (cows) as śruti does not lay down any unequal distribution; this is not accepted and it is proposed that the fee should be distributed in proportion to the work done by each. The established conclusion is that both the views are unacceptable and that the distribution should be in accordance with the meaning of the words [['ardhinah, 31%|'ardhinaḥ,']][^2196] [[trtiyinaḥ|tṛtīyinaḥ]] and [[padinah'|pādinaḥ']] employed by [[Sruti.|Śruti.]]

Manu (VIII. 210) mentions the method of the distribution of fees in Vedic sacrifices outlined above and extends [[ita prin ciples|its principles]] of division to men working jointly on such undertakings as the construction of houses &c.[^2197] Though the sūtra (samam syād-aśrutivāt) is only the pūrvapakṣa view and not [[acooptad|accepted]] as to the distribution of the cows as fees in Vedic sacrifices, it has been frequently followed as an equitable rule by medieval Dharmaśāstra writers. The [[Sm. O.|Sm. C.]] relies on it about the liability of sureties (II. p. 152), about equal shares between father and sons in partition (II. p. 260) and [[U. 285|II. 285]] (distribution of yautaka among unmarried daughters), [[II, 404.|II. 404.]] Kullūka on Manu [[II. 1|III. 1]] (where 36 years are laid down as the period of studentship for mastering the three Vedas) states, relying on the sūtra 'samam' [[&o.|&c.]] that thirty-six years should be equally distributed over the three [[Vedasākhas.|Vedaśākhās.]] The Madanaratna (on Vyavahāra) [[p. 20%|p. 202]]

If there be 112 cows, 28 cows are to be assigned to each of four pofs (viz. mural, अध्वर्युवर्ग, उदातृवर्ग and अपन), then होतवर्ग share is to be divided into as and ng is to get 12 out of 25 parts and bis assistants 6, 4, 3 respectively 1. c. the shares would approzimately be 13, 6, 5, 4 (in 28 cows ).|सर्वेषामर्धिनो मुख्यास्तदर्धेनार्धिनोऽपरे । ... अनेन विधियोगेन कर्तव्यांशप्रकल्पना ॥ मनु VIII. 210-211; एतत्सत्त्वंशपरिकल्पनविधानं तस्य द्वादशशतं दक्षिणेति क्रतुसम्बन्धमात्रेण विहितायां दक्षिणायां न तु ऋत्विग्विशेषसम्बन्धित्वेन विहितायाम् । अयं च दक्षिणानिविदो शङ्के इति तत्प्रतिपादक श्रुतिविरोधापत्तेः । मदनरत्न (व्यव०) pp. 202-203. मदनरत्न adds (p. 204) पशुबन्धादौ विषमविभागो नोक्त इति तत्र साम्येन दक्षिणाविभागः ।'. If there be 112 cows, 28 cows are to be assigned to each of four वर्गs (viz. होतृवर्ग, अध्वर्युवर्ग, उद्गातृवर्ग and ब्रह्मवर्ग), then होतृवर्ग share is to be divided into 25 and होता is to get 12 out of 25 parts and his assistants 6, 4, 3 respectively i. e. the shares would approximately be 13, 6, 5, 4 (in 28 cows).]]

[[P1331]]quotes both verses of Manu (VIII. 210–11), then quotes Manu [[VII, 210|VIII. 210]] (on p. 213) and explains and then sets out the distribution of the dakṣiṇā if it was 112 cows. The [[Vyahāraprakāśa|Vyavahāraprakāśa]] pp. [[449|443]] and 548 (on division of mother's yautaka strīdhana by unmarried daughters) [[refer|refers]] to the rule of equal division. Most of [[those|these]] works (as printed) read the sūtra as [['samam syādasru tatvāt'|'samam syādaśrutatvāt']] and not as Jaimini and [[Sabara|Śabara]] read.

The eleventh chapter deals with tantra which embraces [[Cases|cases]] where one [[aot|act]] serves the purpose of several other acts or [[rites. 2198|rites.]][^2198] For example, there are three yāgas viz. [[purodāsa|puroḍāśa]] (cake) baked on eight potsherds for Agni, [[ourds|curds]] for Indra and milk for Indra; one performance of the Prayājas serves these three (XI. 1. 5–19 and XI. 1. 29–37). [[Adhana (|Ādhāna (]]establishment of the sacred fires) is to be done only once and is not to be repeated in each [[iṣti,|iṣṭi,]] [[pasuyaga|paśuyāga]] or somayāga (XI. 3. 2); the utensils required for Śrauta rites are prepared only once and are to be kept till the death of the sacrificer (XI. 3. [[34-42). 2199|34–42).]][^2199] All these are [[oases|cases]] of tantra. The general rule is that the place, time and the [[per former|performer]] as regards all principal matters in one composite rite such as [[Agneya|Āgneya]] and others in Darśapūrṇamāsa are one (XI. [[%. 1)|2. 1)]] and also that they are the same for [[angas|aṅgas]] (details); but in the case of the [[angas|aṅgas]] (details) the place, time and the performer may be different on account of express texts.

If the fruit or result (phala) follows from all the component sacrifices collectively, then the subsidiary details have to be

[^2198]: [[यत्सत्कृतं बडूनामुपकरोति बत् तन्त्रमित्युच्यते यथा पहना नाह्मणानां मध्ये कृतः प्रदीपः। यस्त्वावल्योपकरोति स आषापः। यथा तेषामेव ब्राह्मणानामहलेपनम् । श्लोकम प्युवाहरन्ति-साधारणं भवेतन्वं परार्थे खमयोजकः। एवमेव प्रसङ्ग स्थाविद्यमाने खके विषो। शबरon XI. 1.1; against repeating such acts as प्रयाजद, शबर remarks 'येन Heat ga: PURIUT Jutfordret a l' on XI. 1.30; even the Mahābhāgya of Patañjali mentions this role ; on alfa 4 (primera mara संमत्ययो यथा लोके) on पा. VI. 1. 84 it remarks 'यथा लोके वसन्ते प्रामणोऽग्री भावधीतेति सकदापाय कृतः शालार्थ इति कुखा पुनः प्रवृत्तिर्न भवति। यथा गर्भाधने पाहाण उपनेय इति सदुपनीय कृतः शालार्थ इति कृत्वा पुनः प्रतिर्न भवति।।|यत्सकृत्कृतं बहूनामुपकरोति तत् तन्त्रमित्युच्यते यथा बहूनां ब्राह्मणानां मध्ये कृतः प्रदीपः । यस्त्वान्वाच्यमुपकरोति स आवापः । यथा तेषामेव ब्राह्मणानामभ्यञ्जनम् । श्लोकमप्युदाहरन्ति—साधारणं भवेत्तन्त्रं परार्थे स्वप्रयोजकः । एवमेव प्रसङ्गः स्याद्विद्यमाने स्वके विधौ ॥ शबर on XI. 1. 1; against repeating such acts as प्रयाजs, शबर remarks 'येन सकृत्कृतेन नोपकृतं स पुनः क्रियमाण उपकरिष्यतीति काशा ।' on XI. 1. 30; even the Mahābhāṣya of Patañjali mentions this rule; on वार्तिक 4 (तत्राव्यये शब्दार्थसंप्रत्ययो यथा लोके) on पा. VI. 1. 84 it remarks 'यथा लोके वसन्ते ब्राह्मणोऽग्नीनादधीतेति सकृदाधाय कृतः शास्त्रार्थ इति कृत्वा पुनः प्रवृत्तिर्न भवति । यथा गर्भाष्टमे ब्राह्मण उपनेय इति सकृदुपनीय कृतः शास्त्रार्थ इति कृत्वा पुनः प्रवृत्तिर्न भवति ।'.]]

For these and other atensils required in Vedic sacrifices, vide H, of Dh, vol. II. p. 985, 0. 2233 ; vide also g. #ft. XI. 3. 43-45 which establish that the Yajõapātras are to be kept from the day of Agayadheya and that placing them on the body of the sacrificer after bis death is pratipathikarma of the atensils and of the sacred fires, Vide to on . I. 6. 8. 2-3 (स्फ्यश्च कपालानि च etc.). For these and other utensils required in Vedic sacrifices, vide H. of Dh. vol. II. p. 985, n. 2233; vide also पू. मी. सू. XI. 3. 43-45 which establish that the Yajñapātras are to be kept from the day of Agnyādheya and that placing them on the body of the sacrificer after his death is pratipattikarma of the utensils and of the sacred fires. [[P1332]]performed only once and not separately in the [[04se|case]] of each component; this is tantra. But if the result follows from each of the component sacrifices [[geparately,|separately,]] then the subsidiary details should be performed with each one of those [[componente.|components.]] That would be a case of āvāpa ([[decentralisation or goattering).|decentralisation or scattering).]] In the [[Darsapūrṇamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] there are really two [[sets 2200|sets]][^2200] of [[saori fices,|sacrifices,]] one called [[Darsa (on amavasya)|Darśa (on amāvāsyā)]] and the other [[Parnamase.|Pūrṇamāsa.]] The subsidiaries prescribed for each are almost the same. Yet they have to be repeated with each of the two sets, the principal reason being that the two are performed on two different days separated by a fortnight, though the two sets together make one single sacrifice leading to a single fruit. Vide PMS XI. [[2, 12-18|2. 12–18]] which is a case of [[ārāpa.|āvāpa.]]

The Aveṣṭi is a sacrifice which is performed as part of the composite sacrifice called [[Rajasūya|Rājasūya]] that could be performed only by Kṣatriyas. It is also an independent sacrifice that could be performed by any one of the three higher [[varpay.|varṇas.]] This is no part of the Rājasūya and is different from it, though the passage about it occurs in the midst of the description of [[Rajasūya. 2201|Rājasūya.]][^2201] This is relied upon by the [[Nirpayasindhu|Nirṇayasindhu]] in connection with the [[Navaratra|Navarātra]] from Āśvina 1st to 9th of the bright half. There were different views, [[vir.|viz.]] [[Devipuja|Devīpūjā]] was to be performed for 9 days or on 8th or 9th tithi (vide above p. 154). The [[Kalika purana|Kālikāpurāṇa]] [[quotes 2202|quotes]][^2202] a verse about [[Devipuja|Devīpūjā]] on the 8th or 9th alone of [[Asvina (sukla)|Āśvina (śukla)]] and the N. S. interprets it as a separate [[wor ship|worship]] on 8th or 9th alone distinct from the whole [[Navaratra.|Navarātra.]]

[^2200]: [[On XI. 2.15 शबर says 'अपि वा न तन्त्रमहानि स्युः । कुतः कर्मपृथकूवात् । तेषां च तग्त्रविधानात् । कर्माणि तावदेतानि भिखानि अग्यः पौर्णमासः समुदायोग्य आमा पास्यः । एवं सर्वत्र । तेषां च देशकालमेवः । पौर्णमास्यां पौर्णमास्या यजेतेत्येवमावि साहनांच तेषां तत्र तत्र देशकालविधिः। ... तस्मात्पौर्णमास्यानां पौर्णमासीकालः । अमावास्यासनाम मावास्याकालः । तत्र गृह्यते विशेषः । विशेषग्रहणाद'.|On XI. 2. 15 शबर says 'अपि वा न तन्त्रमङ्गानि स्युः । कुतः कर्मपृथक्त्वात् । तेषां च तन्त्रविधानात् । कर्माणि तावदेतानि भिन्नानि अन्यः पौर्णमासः समुदायोऽन्य आमावास्यः । एवं सर्वत्र । तेषां च देशकालभेदः । पौर्णमास्यां पौर्णमास्या यजेतेत्येवमादि साङ्गानां च तेषां तत्र तत्र देशकालविधिः । ... तस्मात्पौर्णमास्याङ्गानां पौर्णमासीकालः । अमावास्याङ्गानाममावास्याकालः । तत्र गृह्यते विशेषः । विशेषग्रहणाद्'.]]

II. 3.3; अस्ति राजस्या , राजा राजसूयेन यजेतेति । ते प्रकृत्यामनन्ति-अवेटिं नामेटिम् । आयोऽसाकपालो हिरण्यं दक्षिणा इत्येवमादि । ता प्रकृत्य विधीयते । यदि बाह्मणो पजेत पाईस्परय मध्ये निधायाहति माहुति हुत्वाभिधारयेत् । यदि राजन्य ऐन्वं यदि वैश्यो वैश्वदेवम्-ति। शबर: for पदि ब्राह्मणो ...वैश्वदेवम् vide आप. श्री. 18. 21.11. XI. 4. 8-10.]]

[^2202]: [[एकाइपक्षोपि कालिकापुराणे-यस्त्वेकरयामाग्यो मषम्यामध साधकः पूजये. हरदा देवी महाविभवविस्तरेः । इति। तत्वं राजसवेश्ययागः समप्रधानाया: सदिवाया अप्यवेटेरेतयानायकार्म याजवेदित्येकत्वाग्माये विधानाच यथा फलायों पाहि प्रयोमस्तथा नवरात्रमध्यस्थाया अष्टम्या नवम्याचा फलार्थः पृथक्मयोगः। नि.सि. p. 164.|एकाहपक्षोऽपि कालिकापुराणे—यस्त्वेकस्यामथाष्टम्यां नवम्यामथ साधकः । पूजयेद्वरदां देवीं महाविभवविस्तरैः ॥ इति । तन्नं राजसूयेष्टयागः समप्रधानायाः संदिग्धाया अप्यवेष्टेरेतयान्नाद्यकामं याजयेदित्येकत्वाम्नाये विधानाच्च यथा फलार्थः पृथक् प्रयोगस्तथा नवरात्रमध्यस्थाया अष्टम्या नवम्याश्चापि फलार्थः पृथक्प्रयोगः । नि. सि. p. 164.]]

[[P1333]]The same adhikarana where the Pūrvapakṣa proposes that [[Raja|Rājā]] means any one of any [[varpa|varṇa]] who rules over a kingdom and [[oftere|offers]] protection to the country and its cities, the siddhānta (PMS and [[Sabara)|Śabara)]] holds that 'rājā' is a word expressive of a caste [[Viz.|viz.]] Kṣatriya, and this is referred to by several late [[Dharmasastra|Dharmaśāstra]] works like the Rājadharmakaustubha (p. 5). The V. P. refers to this adhikarana and interprets the verse of Nārada viz. 'one who is an apostate from the order of ascetics would become the [[slavo|slave]] of the king' as providing that even a [[ksatriya|kṣatriya]] apostate would have to be the slave even of a [[vaisya|vaiśya]] ruler, though the word 'rājā' in the primary [[sepse|sense]] means a kṣatriya yet in a secondary [[senge (lakṣanā)|sense (lakṣaṇā)]] any one who protects [[subjets|subjects]] could be called rājā. The Par. M. discusses this adhikarana at great length (I. 1. pp. 449–55). It may be noticed that the meaning of 'rājā' as kṣatriya in early texts changed to any ruler of any class who protects the country and people ruled over by him. This change is brought out briefly in Tantravārtika on III. 5. 26.

The 12th chapter deals with the topics of prasaṅga, vikalpa and the like. Prasaṅga occurs when something done in one place is helpful in another place also, just as when a lamp is lighted in a mansion it illumines the public road also.[^2203] In connection with the [[Agnisomlya|Agniṣomīya]] animal sacrifice an offering of [[pasupurodāsa|paśupuroḍāśa]] (a cake of the meat of the animal sacrificed) in the words 'having offered the omentum of the animal to [[Agoi|Agni]] and Soma one offers [[&|a]] [[pasupurodāsa oooked|paśupuroḍāśa cooked]] on eleven potsherds to Agni and Soma'. The question is whether the details such as prayājas should be performed [[afrosh|afresh]] for this [[ar|or]] whether those details performed at the time of offering the omentum will suffice. The established conclusion is that the details already performed at offering [[pasu (amentum)|paśu (omentum)]] will serve for the offering of the meat [[oake|cake]] also.[^2204] In such cases the place or time and performer are to be the same. This conclusion is relied upon in the [[Prāyasoittaviveka|Prāyaścittaviveka]] which says that when [[&|a]] man performs the expiation for twelve [[year|years]] in connection with grave sins of
